The five named G306 operator-side writes harden to 0600 (donut assembly, the generator_env staged tree, and the token stager's `main.go` plus its `go.mod`). The errcheck typed-wrapper debt (185/146) closes: config exclude groups are recorded with rationales — the gorilla websocket deadline and writer family, the dns/gin/csv/json response writers, the listener `Stop()` teardown family including `(*Server).stopExtraListener`, `BackupManager.PerformBackup` (which journals its own failures), `(*gorm.DB).DB`, multipart test-fixture framing, the windows victim-memory teardown free functions, the `syscall.SyscallN` slot idiom and `fmt.Sscanf` fallback parses — with the documented gotcha that exclude-functions match only by full declaration import path (alias-name entries silently match nothing). About 95 call sites are fixed point by point with explicit `_, _ =` style discards and reasons: agent cleanup helpers (NtFree/NtClose/CloseHandle/cleanup runs), SOCKS handshake writes, SSH session replies, `SetTrustedProxies(nil)` clears, webhook goroutines. The `S1011`/`S1001` one-offs are fixed, so both analysis targets now sit at the pure gosec backlog: 70 windows (carrying the usual +1 CI-observation headroom) / 71 linux, errcheck and staticcheck at zero

- Win TUN/Wintun packaging (2026-10 batch P) makes `tun_start` real on Windows: the implant carries the signed Wintun runtime per architecture and drives it through the official pure-Go binding (`golang.zx2c4.com/wintun`), opening an `fc2tun0` adapter and running the same tun_data framing over the beacon as the Linux `/dev/net/tun` path (addresses applied with `netsh`). The runtime is never committed: `scripts/fetch-wintun.{ps1,sh}` fetch the pinned wintun 0.14.1 ZIP (URL + SHA256 in one place) and stage `wintun.dll` under `internal/payload/agent/assets/wintun/<arch>/`, the generator adds the `wintun` build tag only when the requested target's DLL is staged, and a checkout that never fetched keeps the honest "no bundled Wintun" stub (`TestWintunBuildTags`, `TestExtractAgentAssets`, `TestExtractAgentSourcesStagesFetchedWintun`). Creating the adapter needs an elevated implant, and the failure is honest: the unelevated path returns the raw `Access is denied`/`ERROR_ELEVATION_REQUIRED` plus an explicit "needs an elevated/administrator context" hint, reached only after the real driver install is attempted (verified live on a hardened Windows host, `TestWintunLiveAdapter`). The signature-hardening interaction is handled and pinned: MicrosoftSignedOnly — which the agent's own adaptive evasion applies on most real EDR hosts — refuses any fresh non-Microsoft-signed image with `ERROR_INVALID_IMAGE_HASH` (577), and wintun.dll is WireGuard LLC-signed, so `blockDLLs()` now preloads the bundled runtime (staged next to the implant with a %TEMP% fallback, forced through `wintun.Version()`; best-effort and silent on failure) before it applies the policy; `TestWintunPreloadSurvivesHardening` proves the runtime stays bound while a fresh copy is refused. CI fetches the runtimes ahead of the smoke job — so the amd64/arm64 smoke builds now really compile the tagged embed path — and adds windows cross-compile gates for the untagged stub plus the tagged amd64/arm64/386 matrix. EN/ZH TUN copy, `docs/CAPABILITY_MATRIX.md`, the generated `docs/COMMAND_REFERENCE.md` and the TUN API note now say "Windows: bundled Wintun, needs admin" instead of "not bundled". An elevated lab run on 2026-10-17 (real driver install with the adapter created and removed three times) validated the full path and caught a teardown race the unelevated tests could never reach: `stopAgentTUN` could call `Session.End()` while the read loop was still inside the DLL's `ReceivePacket`, faulting on the freed session (0xc0000005) — teardown now waits for the read loop to leave the DLL (bounded grace) and holds the session lock across `End()`/`Close()` and `tunWritePacket`'s DLL calls, and `TestWintunLiveAdapter` runs three start/stop cycles to keep that class of race caught.

### Security

- **Six 5xx responses echoed raw internal errors, and a gate now prevents it.** The generation handlers answered 500 with `err.Error()` for v3 registration-secret and implant-config failures, and the reverse-port-forward bind error included the raw socket message — all of which can name key material, absolute paths or local addresses. They now log the real error and answer with `sanitizeError`. `scripts/check-error-sanitization.mjs` (`check:error-sanitization` in `npm run check`) walks every `respondError(..., StatusInternalServerError, …)` call and fails when the message is a raw error rather than a literal or a sanitizer; verified in both directions with a probe file. It scanned 1,277 5xx call sites clean after the change.

- Unbound v3 registration secrets now expire at authentication instead of only at the hourly sweep: `server.reg_secret_orphan_ttl_hours` (default 168h = 7 days, hot-reloadable) bounds an unbound build secret, and the auth path refuses one past that age even when the tick has not fired yet, so a leaked build secret cannot enroll an agent long after its build. The sweep also runs once at startup rather than only after the first hour. Secrets already bound to a deployed agent never expire — they are that agent's long-term credential, and deleting one would permanently lock the beacon out
- Hot update refuses to install a release it cannot prove is newer: version comparison now fails closed when either version is not a plain numeric release stamp (a `dev`/nightly build, a build whose version was never injected, or a tag with a prerelease suffix), instead of treating the unreadable side as "older" and accepting an arbitrary published release
- Agent no longer downgrades to cleartext when every configured C2 URL is encrypted: the WSS→HTTP fallback, the DNS→HTTP fallback and transport failover/rotation refuse `http`/`ws`/`h2c`/`tcp`/`udp`/`dns` for such an implant (configure at least one cleartext URL to keep lab failover)
- HTTP(S)/mTLS beacon clients refuse a redirect from an encrypted origin to `http://`; cleartext lab origins keep normal redirect behaviour
- Agent certificate pinning now actually authenticates self-signed teamserver certificates: the pin replaces chain validation (plus a hostname check when a server name is known) instead of failing before the pin is consulted
- Agent DNS answers are validated before use: response bit, rcode, transaction ID, question name and question type must match the outstanding query
- Agent beacon responses are size-capped (16 MiB) so a hostile endpoint cannot make the implant buffer unbounded data
- Operator-plane guard now covers the authenticated `/api/v1` REST surface (only `/api/v1/health` is exempt for probes); `operator_allowed_cidrs` and operator mTLS can no longer be bypassed via API-key/session automation routes
- Audit logs are tenant-scoped: `AuditLog.TenantID` is stamped on write, and both audit read surfaces (operator page + `/api/v1/audit`) plus retention purge return the caller's own tenant plus legacy `tenant_id = 0` rows; the tamper-evident hash chain input is unchanged so historical `EntryHash` values stay verifiable
- gRPC mTLS is fail-closed: a missing/unreadable/unparseable `client_ca_file` with `require_client_cert` refuses to start the listener instead of silently serving without client authentication
- Per-listener TLS fails closed instead of degrading: `require_client_cert` without a client CA (per listener or server-wide), an unreadable or certificate-less `client_ca_file`, a `min_tls_version` other than 1.2/1.3, or TLS knobs on a non-TLS scheme are refused with `400` at the API boundary and refuse the listener start, so a row can never describe a listener that would come up without the check the operator asked for; an extra HTTPS listener whose TLS configuration cannot be honoured is no longer handed to the accept loop at all (the failure used to be logged asynchronously after the listener row was already recorded as running)
- Beacon resync no longer answers unauthenticated frames while the server still holds that agent's session (UUID-existence oracle / response amplifier); AEAD-verified replays and genuinely lost sessions still receive the resync + rekey
- Plugin execution is fail-closed: a process guard that cannot be attached now kills the plugin and refuses the run, `Cmd.WaitDelay` bounds `Wait` when a grandchild holds the output pipes, the guard is released as soon as the context fires, and manifest timeouts are clamped to a 300s server ceiling
- Goja scripts can no longer `require()` host modules (deny-all source loader; `console` still available), script source is capped at 1 MiB, and top-level/event execution is time-bounded (10s parse / 30s callback)
- GitHub Actions are pinned to immutable commit SHAs and every Dockerfile/compose base image to a multi-arch digest (the human version stays in a comment and Dependabot still raises update PRs), removing the mutable-tag supply-chain gap
- Live screen frames no longer reach read-only roles through the notification socket: the dashboard `/ws` screenshot fan-out is filtered by the same gate as the screenshot HTTP APIs (operator identity plus `agents.write`, custom roles resolved through the role table, unknown users fail closed). Previously the tenant check passed a user whose role could never fetch a frame over HTTP, so a read-only account in the same tenant could watch a desktop by holding a socket
- Remote-desktop input is validated and rate-limited at the API boundary instead of being handed to the agent unread: types are whitelisted to `move`/`click`/`key`, coordinates are bounded to ±32767, key names to 1–32 characters, and accepted events are capped at 40 per second per (operator, agent) with an explicit `429`. Every accepted event writes a task row plus an audit row, so an unbounded client was a write-amplification primitive, not just an input flood. The legacy `/ws/remote-desktop` relay applies the same normalization plus a per-connection budget, and the screen-stream `width` parameter is validated (16–16384) and canonicalized before it is spliced into the agent's CSV stream command — an arbitrary string there was parameter injection into the agent-side parser; an unknown `mime` is a `400` instead of being silently ignored, and `mime` without `width` is refused because the agent parses that command positionally
- The legacy `/ws/remote-desktop` upgrade is hardened end to end: the browser origin is checked against the configured allowlist (browsers attach session cookies to cross-site upgrades), the operator key comes from the authenticated session instead of a spoofable query parameter, per-agent (8) and global (64) connection caps are enforced before the upgrade so refusals are real HTTP statuses, a second stream from the same operator replaces and closes the stale socket, and open/close are written to the audit trail. `/rd/:id/screenshot` also records an audit row now
- Agent-scoped pages are tenant-scoped: the screen-monitor page and the token-management page looked up the agent without a tenant filter, so an operator in one tenant could render another tenant's agent page/JSON (identity fields, token entries) by id; both directions now use the scoped lookup and redirect instead of leaking
- mTLS client material is validated before a build starts: a certificate without its key (or vice versa), a pair that does not parse as `tls.X509KeyPair`, a CA bundle containing no PEM certificates, or any single entry over 64 KiB is refused with `400` at the API boundary instead of being embedded into a binary that could never complete the handshake
- The token stager's client private key cannot be read off the build process: the mTLS material is compiled from a generated source file instead of `-X` ldflags (a PEM block also cannot survive `-X`, whose value cannot carry newlines), matching the config-blob rule the agent build already follows; a stager whose material does not parse exits before the fetch instead of silently downgrading to an unauthenticated one

### Fixed

- Two operator-facing pages no longer crash on real data. `/report` read the task section's `stats` field — a single aggregate object — as if it were a list and called `.map` on it, so the Report page blanked behind the error boundary on any deployment with tasks; the section API's raw task rows are now aggregated by type (`aggregateTaskStats`), every list section tolerates a `null` envelope, and the summary tiles read the dashboard API instead of the `/report` HTML route (which answers with the page's own context, not counters). `/circuit-breaker` dereferenced `fail_reasons.length` on a listener whose slice Go had encoded as `null`, so the Circuit Breaker page failed the same way whenever a monitored listener had no recorded reason. Both pages have regression tests.
- Default buttons are no longer dark-on-dark. `tailwind-merge` did not know the dense-console size ramp (`text-compact`, `text-micro`, …) and classified them as text colours, so it deleted the foreground colour that sat next to them — `bg-primary text-primary-foreground text-compact` lost `text-primary-foreground` and every default button in the app rendered as black text on a black surface. `cn()` now registers the eight custom sizes with `extendTailwindMerge`, and the merge behaviour is pinned by unit tests.
- Timestamps that never happened no longer render as if they had. Go's zero time (`0001-01-01`) reached the UI as "1/1/1, 8:05:43 AM" or "20873d ago" — a listener that was never probed reported a probe time, and a beacon that never checked in reported a 56-year-old age. `isZeroTime()` recognises the placeholder, `timeAgo()` falls back to "unknown" for it, unprobed listeners read **Never**, and raw ISO strings on the listener detail page (created-at, last-seen) are formatted like the rest of the console.
- The sidebar's Listeners badge now agrees with the Listeners page. The dashboard counter for listeners is deployment-global while the list endpoint is tenant-scoped, so a tenant-bound operator saw a badge of 2 over a table of 1 (a legacy listener with no tenant stamp is running but is not manageable by that tenant). The badge reads the tenant-scoped total instead, matching what the operator can actually see and manage. No API, route or permission changed.
- PE finalization and validation are correct again: the data-directory base is now `e_lfanew + 0x18 + (0x60 PE32 | 0x70 PE32+)` instead of `e_lfanew + 0x60/0x70`, so stripping the debug directory no longer zeroes the `.pdata` exception directory and leaves the real debug entry behind; `ApplyTimestamp` reads the full 32-bit `e_lfanew`. Generated EXE/DLL artifacts are validated for machine/optional-header magic, EXE/DLL flag, subsystem, entry point, section bounds and alignment, checksum, certificate-table placement and (when requested) signature validity; a requested-but-unavailable icon/preset/disguise now fails the build instead of silently shipping the default Go icon, and VersionInfo is emitted even for icon-less builds. `OriginalFilename` now carries the operator-visible name without the server's 8-hex uniqueness prefix and falls back to a neutral `agent.exe`/`agent.dll` rather than the internal build name. Authenticode hashing was aligned with WinVerifyTrust: the PE checksum field and security-directory entry are omitted rather than hashed as zeros, and the PKCS#7 `messageDigest` covers the contents octets of `SpcIndirectDataContent` (not its full TLV), eliminating the `TRUST_E_BAD_DIGEST`/`HashMismatch` results seen against real Windows verifiers.
- Windows `pe_imports` mimicry now produces loadable PEs again. The old no-slack fallback spliced bytes into `.idata`, shifted every later section and the COFF string table while leaving `PointerToSymbolTable` at its old offset (so `debug/pe` failed with `unexpected EOF`), and grew `VirtualSize` without checking the next section. Import injection now (a) runs each in-place/relocated attempt on a copy, (b) keeps the relocated table clear of the old mapped region so the existing ILT's terminating null thunk is not overwritten, and (c) appends a `.idata2` section holding a complete descriptor array/IAT instead of shifting existing bytes when there is no safe slack; the COFF symbol pointer, raw section offsets and virtual ranges stay untouched, and a PE that already carries a certificate table is refused rather than modified into an invalid signature. Validation now walks every import descriptor, DLL name, ILT/IAT thunk and hint/name inside mapped section data and rejects section virtual-range overlaps, `TestSmokeWindowsPEOptions` is part of the CI smoke matrix, section renaming matches resolved names (including COFF `/N` long names) instead of table indexes, and `random` PE timestamps are drawn from 2019-01-01 to yesterday rather than a future date.
- Masked and evasive sleeps are now interruptible by the same wake path as a normal sleep: `sleepWithMask` ran a single `BeforeSleep` for the whole interval (potentially hours) and the Windows evasion chunks slept without checking the wake channel, so a confirmed network change, ghost transition or kill-date woke the monitor but the beacon loop still had to wait out the sleep. Masked sleeps now run in 5 s chunks inside one encrypt/decrypt window (the config buffer never leaves the encrypted state between chunks), and the evasive sleep checks the wake channel after each chunk (the non-Windows fallback uses the interruptible helper); an early return still decrypts exactly once. Tests pin the 5 s/5 s/1 s split for an 11 s sleep, the one-chunk early return, and the Windows/non-Windows evasion wake
- Agent state files are now crash-atomic: `beacon.seq`, the registration marker, `agent.uuid`, the persistent identity key, the pinned update key and the delivered network config were written in place with `os.WriteFile`, so a power cut or a kill during a write could leave a zero-length or partially written file. The consequences ranged from a sequence reset (server replay rejection and a resync handshake), through a lost operator endpoint (the agent falls back to a dead build-time C2) and a lost update trust root, to a **regenerated identity key** whose result-spool encryption no longer matches. Each is now written to a same-directory temp file, flushed, and renamed into place (directory `fsync` on Unix, hidden after rename on Windows), so a crash leaves the previous or the new value, never a truncated one. The registration marker is only rewritten when it actually changed (it used to be rewritten on every beacon), the identity key is synced before first use, and startup sweeps stale `.state-*.tmp` / `.results-*.tmp` files so a crashed write cannot leave the private key in a stray temp file. Tests pin atomic replacement without leftovers, 0600 mode on Unix, and the temp sweep
- Pending task output now survives a crash or restart instead of dying with the process: results are snapshotted to an AES-256-GCM `results.spool` in the agent state directory (key HKDF-derived from the persistent X25519 identity key and bound to the agent UUID through AAD, so a copied file from another agent/host does not decrypt), written atomically (temp file + `fsync` + rename, hidden on Windows) and debounced by one second so keylogger/stream bursts coalesce into a single write. The snapshot keeps the in-flight beacon's results until the reply decrypts, so a kill mid-beacon replays them rather than losing them (the ResultID makes the replay idempotent); decoding a successful reply shrinks the spool, and an empty queue deletes the file. Startup restores and re-bounds the queue (1024 results / 16 MiB output, oldest dropped and counted in `dropped_results`), assigns ResultIDs at enqueue time so restored results keep their idempotency key, and discards a spool that fails authentication (rotated identity, copy from another agent, corruption) instead of retrying it forever. Task acks are deliberately not persisted: agent restarts still redeliver unacknowledged tasks, matching the existing at-least-once semantics. Tests cover the encrypted round trip, tamper/wrong-identity rejection, empty-snapshot deletion, load/corruption handling, in-flight snapshot composition and requeue behaviour, plus the bounds re-check. A failed quick send (`sendTaskResult`) now flushes synchronously as well, so a `kill`/`uninstall`/`migrate` result queued moments before the process exits is on disk instead of being cut off by the debounce
- Raw-transport beacons now use the whole multi-C2 list: TCP/TLS, UDP, QUIC and ICMP dialed only the active endpoint, so a `tcp://primary,tcp://backup` artifact silently behaved like a single-endpoint config — the cross-transport rotator switches protocol, not endpoint, and with a one-transport candidate list it is a no-op. A shared sweep now walks the published URL list in failover order (active endpoint first, then every entry whose scheme the transport speaks: `tcp`/`tls`, `udp`, `quic`, `icmp`), remembers the endpoint that answered so the next beacon starts there, and leaves the index untouched when the whole sweep fails. Foreign schemes are never reinterpreted (an `https://` entry is not fed to the TCP framer), except that the active endpoint is always attempted even when its scheme does not match, preserving single-URL configs where the protocol was set explicitly. DNS keeps its dedicated `dns.domain`/`dns.server` config and SMB its local pipe/socket
- Long-running beacons now refresh their live network config without re-registering: an encrypted beacon response carries the same secret-bound blob (`network_config`) when the assembled config (listener endpoint/protocol, malleable profile/pools, server-tracked sleep/jitter) changed since the last delivery, or when the last delivery is older than 15 minutes — the periodic resend heals a dropped frame, a server restart or a stale persisted blob. Datagram transports (UDP/ICMP/DNS) skip the blob when it cannot fit one response frame rather than emitting an oversized packet, and an unchanged periodic refresh is a no-op on the agent (fingerprint compare: no disk rewrite, no network reparse). Wire-compatible: the field is `omitempty` and v2/master-key agents never receive it. The registration/handshake path keeps delivering the config as before
- Beacon rate limiting is observable: every 429 from the per-IP limiter increments `forgec2_beacon_rate_limited_total` (via a limiter callback, so the middleware package stays free of Prometheus), which makes a NAT/fleet sharing one IP or an actual flood distinguishable from healthy traffic. The default stays 100 requests/60 s/IP and the 429 keeps both the `Retry-After` header and the JSON `retry_after` field
- An implant that changes networks no longer sleeps through the change: the agent polls a sorted fingerprint of its non-loopback interfaces/addresses every 20 s and wakes the beacon loop when a new state is confirmed on two consecutive polls (the same wake path as ghost/kill-date transitions, now also observed by the JIT-scheduler sleep on Windows). Requiring confirmation keeps a flapping link from shortening the sleep to the poll interval, and confirmed wakes are additionally capped at one per minute. Transient interface-query errors (empty fingerprint) never count as a change, so they cannot cause a wake storm. This complements the existing post-sleep scheduler trigger, which only noticed a change after the interval had already elapsed
- A host whose clock is more than the server's five-minute tolerance away (fresh VM clone, dead CMOS battery, NTP not yet converged) used to fail registration forever with no way to learn the correct time. The agent now reads the `Date` header of every HTTP response — including the rejection itself — and stamps registration, handshake and ciphertext frames with the implied server-clock offset. The offset is recomputed from each response rather than accumulated, so fixing the host clock (or NTP catching up) brings it back to zero on the next reply. Reverse proxies must pass `Date` through; the recovery covers HTTP(S) and the WSS handshake, while raw TCP/SSH/SMB listeners carry no HTTP clock
- Beacon backpressure is treated as backpressure, not as a dead session: any non-200 used to invalidate the ECDH session and force fast mode, so a 429 or a 5xx from the default 100 requests/60 s/IP limiter produced a reconnect/handshake storm, and a 200 carrying an HTML captive-portal/proxy page was counted as success and cleared the failure counter. `sendToC2` now classifies the response: only 400 resets the session (the teamserver's invalid/unknown-frame status), 408/429/5xx stay retryable, and a standard `Retry-After` header (seconds or HTTP-date, capped at five minutes) becomes a floor for the next backoff without being reduced by jitter. A 200 is accepted only when the body is valid JSON after malleable decoding, so an invalid 200 keeps the failure counter and the pending results; results/acks that were in an encrypted frame and did not receive a valid response are re-queued (ResultID keeps server-side replay idempotent), while registration/handshake frames requeue their payload before transmission because those frames carry no task body. A register frame that receives 400 switches to the authenticated handshake path instead of replaying register forever, and the `registered` marker file is now removed when the server asks for re-registration rather than resurrecting the stale local state on the next start. The beacon rate limiter also returns the standard `Retry-After` header alongside its existing JSON `retry_after` field. Tests cover status classification, `Retry-After` parsing/clamping, invalid-200 rejection, pending-result requeueing and marker removal.
- Multi-C2 failover survives generation and registration: the Generate page's `c2_url` list was authoritative only in the browser. The server replaced it with the listener-resolved primary (`form.C2URL = resolved.C2URL`) on the binary, Go one-liner and stager paths, the agent then replaced the compile-time list with the single URL delivered in the registration network config, and the persisted config was applied *before* the build-time list was parsed. The resolved primary is now authoritative while every operator-supplied failover entry is validated, deduplicated and preserved (bare `host:port` defaults to `http://`; unsupported schemes/credentials/query strings are rejected with 400); the agent merges a server-delivered primary over the previous first entry instead of resetting the chain, and the persisted config is applied only after the compile-time list exists. The stager's stage-1 fetch uses the primary origin while stage-2 keeps the full list, and the network-config scheme allowlist now covers the transports the generator can actually emit (`wss`, `h2c`, `grpc(s)`, `quic`, `mtls`, …). The PowerShell one-liner remains single-endpoint because its template dials exactly one URL; Go-built artifacts carry the chain. Tests cover list merge/validation, binary and stager form preservation, primary-origin extraction and agent-side failover retention.
- The console's entire font-size ramp is real again: `text-(--fs-*)` was never a font-size utility — Tailwind compiles `text-<value>` to `color:` unless the name resolves from a `--text-<name>` theme entry, so `text-(--fs-compact)` emitted `color: var(--fs-compact)`, a length in a colour slot that the browser discards without warning. All 360 call sites in 113 files silently inherited 14/15px. Browser-measured on `/agents`: page titles rendered at 15px instead of 24px, table headers at 14px instead of 10px (erasing the header/cell hierarchy), badges and `kbd` chips at 14px instead of 9–10px, card section titles at 15px instead of 18px. The ramp is now declared in `@theme inline` as `--text-micro` … `--text-prose`, each pointing at its `--fs-*` token so `[data-density="compact"]` actually cascades, and the sites were migrated by `scripts/migrate-fs-utilities.mjs`; new `check-design-tokens.mjs` Rules 5/6 fail the build on the broken spelling or on a missing `--text-*` declaration, so the defect cannot return quietly
- Empty states no longer clip their message inside a table: `EmptyState` is routinely rendered in a `TableCell`, which sets `whitespace-nowrap` for data cells, so the status line refused to wrap and was cut mid-sentence ("Deploy your first implant to establish a…") on every table-backed page — measured 360px of text in a 320px box at 1280 and 371 in 320 at 390; the primitive now sets `whitespace-normal` + `text-balance`, and a 10-route × 4-viewport runtime probe reports zero clipped text nodes
- Focus mode no longer occludes the page title: `[data-focus="on"] #main-content { padding-top: 0 }` assumed the top bar was hidden with the rest of the shell, but the bar is `fixed` and stays, so the heading sat entirely underneath it (measured: heading top 28px, bar bottom 56px); the padding stays and focus mode instead reclaims the 224px sidebar column plus the breadcrumb strip
- Dashboard stat tiles align on one baseline: one- and two-word labels in the same grid wrapped to different heights, stair-stepping the values 35px apart (`BEACONS` one line vs `QUEUED TASKS` two). The tile reserves a two-line label slot and pushes its trend row down with `mt-auto`
- Compact density now compacts tables: `[data-density="compact"] .agent-table …` targeted a class no component ever rendered, so the mode changed the type ramp and nothing else. The rules key off the table primitive's own `data-slot` markers instead
- Global navigation is reachable on touch devices: the command-palette trigger was `hidden … lg:flex` and the only other door was Ctrl+K, while on a phone the sidebar is a closed sheet — so there was no entry point to the palette at all. A 44px icon trigger now appears below `lg`
- Sub-32px touch targets are gone: `SectionCard`'s "View all" link was a bare `text-xs` anchor (44×16px) on every dashboard panel, and the top-bar status pill was 16px tall. Both are now ≥32px hit areas (the pill is a real focusable button with a focus ring). Measured on `/dashboard` at 390px with touch emulation: small-target count 9 → 0
- The connection state reads as text at every width: the top-bar label was `hidden … lg:inline`, so below `lg` live/offline/reconnecting was a bare colour dot (WCAG 1.4.1 use-of-colour). The label is always rendered now, in a fixed minimum-width slot so switching states cannot shift the row, and a layout assertion fails if it is ever hidden again. The overflow sweep also grows to the top-10 operator routes × 4 viewports (adds `loot`, `credentials`, `generate`)
- 140 `-foreground/100` no-op opacity modifiers (`text-muted-foreground/100` is identical to the unmodified class) removed across 46 files, so genuine opacity decisions are no longer buried in noise
- The token-vault page no longer crashes on an unexpected response envelope: it read the payload as `tokenData.tokens || tokenData.data || tokenData`, so a body carrying neither key fell back to the *envelope object* and the subsequent `.filter(...)` threw `TypeError: x.filter is not a function`, taking the whole page down through the ErrorBoundary; the same shape existed on the NTLM page (`agentsData?.agents || agentsData`, then `.map`). Both go through the total `firstArray` helper, which keeps the key priority but always yields an array. Found by the widened route sweep — the e2e mock envelope happens to satisfy the old keys, so neither crash fired under test
- Fixed-width columns no longer clip unbounded identifiers: the toolkit page pinned task-type ids to `w-28`, so `screen_trigger_start` (144px of text) was cut off in a 112px cell — the column now sizes to content and the description truncates instead; the scripting page's API reference rendered long signatures (`query('agents'|'tasks'|…)`, 374px) in an unwrapped block that overflowed its card at both 1280 and 390, and now wraps
- Visual baselines no longer capture a half-rendered page: `waitForPageSurface` now waits for `networkidle` before settling document height, which removes the intermittent 3–4% pixel diffs on `/ai` where a lazy chunk landed between the settle and the capture; `/chat` left `VISUAL_ROUTES` because it is a client-side redirect to `/ai` (same surface under a second name, and capturing mid-redirect produced duplicated-content baselines)
- Test files and tooling can no longer leak into the production stylesheet: Tailwind's scanner reads every word, so batch V's `useForm.test.tsx` blur calls emitted a real `.blur` utility, which renamed the entry CSS and cascaded through the entry chunk, every app chunk and all 114 route HTMLs — 426 embedded-bundle diffs that reddened the CI webdist gate (a clean local rebuild reproduced it exactly; the stale `frontend/out` had hidden it, because no rebuild followed the test batch). `globals.css` now excludes `*.test.*` / `*.spec.*` (under `src/`), `e2e/` and `scripts/` from the scan, and the resynced `internal/webdist/dist` drops the six phantom utilities those directories had been feeding — `.row-0` and `.row-119` (a table test's fixture strings), `.w-3`, `.w-4`, `.shadow-[...]` (the design-token checker's own example literal) and `.[api:poll]`; a selector-set diff of the entry CSS shows six removals and zero additions
- WASM scaffolds and the shipped example link with a plain clang again: clang lowers copy loops to `memcpy`/`memset` even under `-nostdlib`, so wasm-ld refused the module with `undefined symbol: memcpy` (zig hid the problem by linking its own runtime). The C skeletons now define those two shims themselves and build with `-ffreestanding`, `docs/PLUGIN_TIERS.md` spells the requirement out, and the example's committed `plugin.wasm` was rebuilt from the fixed source
- WASM plugins can no longer have their own memory overwritten by the host: the input JSON is written past the module's initial linear memory instead of at a fixed `1024` offset — which is exactly where wasm-ld's default layout puts the data segment of a real C/Rust/TinyGo module — so a module's globals, heap or stack survive until `run()` reads the input through the pointer argument
- WASM plugins that define no linear memory (or do not export `memory`) are refused with an explicit error instead of crashing the host on wazero's typed-nil `Module.Memory()` interface; the `forgec2.log` import has the same guard
- WASM ABI v1 returns the reply as **one `i64`** (low 32 bits pointer, high 32 bits length) instead of two `i32` results: building the shipped example with a real toolchain showed that clang lowers a two-field struct return to an indirect (sret) parameter even with `+multivalue`, so the documented multi-value result was not reachable from ordinary C/Rust code. The host now rejects a module whose `run` returns anything else, with an explicit error
- WebSocket batch retries no longer silently drop an oversized result: the frame being retried is always retained and only the backlog is trimmed to the 8 MiB cap, so an agent can no longer wait forever on a reply that was discarded
- `find` and `reg_set` task dispatch no longer returns `400 task type requires 'command' parameter` for valid requests: both now populate the command slot the protocol metadata requires (`find` falls back to the search root when no pattern is supplied, `reg_set` carries `path=value`)
- Fresh installs from `config.example.yaml` boot again: missing `crypto.loot_key` / `extc2_key` / `backup_key` / `totp_key` / `csrf_key` are generated at startup and persisted (idempotent, env vars win, read-only configs keep working with a warning)
- `FORGEC2_DATA_DIR` now rebases `data_dir` and every derived path (DB, TLS cert/key, SSH host key); compose sets it to `/data` so state and logs live on the persistent volume instead of the tmpfs working directory
- Base compose binds `0.0.0.0` by default, so published ports are actually reachable
- Prometheus request-duration labels use the route template (`unmatched` for NoRoute) instead of the raw URL path, removing high-cardinality series and per-request id leakage
- `scripts/e2e-c-implant.bat` could never report a task result: the poll-timeout branch had unescaped parentheses inside an `if` block, so `goto :CLEANUP_FAIL` ran on every invocation regardless of the task outcome (and the `timeout` wait failed outright under redirected stdin, so the loop never actually waited); the block is fixed, the wait uses `ping`, login now validates the HTTP status before blaming the CSRF cookie, poll responses are read as UTF-8 (a non-ASCII result no longer breaks parsing), a failed task exits 1 with the server's own error text, and an eighth argument or `E2E_EXTRA_JSON` appends raw JSON task fields — documented as JSON-escaped, because a single `\U` is not a valid JSON escape and the server answers `invalid request`
- `POST /api/v1/tasks` answers `400` naming the failing parameter instead of an opaque `500 "task creation failed"`: request-shaped failures (missing mandatory parameter, unknown type, RoE/OPSEC refusal, queue/lock limits, oversized command, soft-lock conflicts) are typed request errors now, while genuinely unexpected faults are logged at error level before the generic 500. `read` takes the file path in the `command` slot, so `{"type":"read","path":...}` answers `task type read requires 'command' parameter` — the wording the new Go-agent E2E gate asserts
- Extra `tcp://`/`tls://` listeners no longer take the server down on their first connection: the connection goroutine ran the WaitGroup `Done()` twice (the accept loop's wrapper plus `handleTCPConnection`), so every connection — including a health probe — decremented the counter and could end in `sync: negative WaitGroup counter`, which panics the process; the wrapper now hands the accounting to the handler exactly like the main listener does
- Listener health no longer reports a healthy mTLS listener as broken: the probe presents no client certificate, so a TLS handshake against a listener with `require_client_cert` could only fail and turned a healthy row into a failure with alerting; those rows now get a TCP reachability check and are reported as `skipped` with the reason surfaced, while a port that is actually down still fails as before
- The remote-desktop page can start a stream again: it posted the poll interval in milliseconds (250–2000) while the server accepts 1–60 seconds, so every fresh start was answered `400 interval must be between 1 and 60 seconds` and the page only appeared to work when a monitor was already running from the Screen tab; the hook now converts to seconds and clamps to the server's floor
- Remote-desktop websocket sessions no longer expire after five minutes of operator silence: the read deadline only extends on pongs and the server never sent pings, so the socket died mid-session with no way for a browser to keep it alive (the WebSocket API cannot emit pings); a keepalive ticker pings every 30s while the 5-minute deadline remains as a liveness bound for genuinely dead peers
- Screen-monitor start/stop no longer holds the registry mutex across task creation, plugin hooks, task broadcasts and audit writes — a frame ingest takes the same lock on every `screen_frame`, so an unrelated DB insert could stall live frames. A single-flight reservation replaces the lock-hold: a concurrent start/stop for the same agent is answered `409` instead of silently double-queueing, and a failed task creation releases the reservation
- Generated mTLS agents now actually present their client certificate: `initMTLS` fed the base64-encoded `main.MTLSCertStr` / `MTLSKeyStr` / `MTLSCAStr` values straight into `X509KeyPair` without decoding, so material delivered in the documented format failed to parse, `mtlsCertLoaded` stayed false and the agent connected with no certificate — a listener with `require_client_cert` rejected the beacon even though the build "had" the certificate, and the failure was only visible in debug builds. The values are decoded now (base64 first, raw PEM accepted as a fallback for hand-built binaries), and the whole path is pinned by a real uTLS handshake test: a server that requires and verifies a client certificate accepts the agent, the same server refuses it without one, and garbage material fails closed without loading a half-initialized state
- Token stagers generated from the form now honour the transport settings they were always given: `skip_tls_verify`, `proxy` and `user_agent` were parsed into the stager form but never copied into the fetch descriptor, so a stager built for a self-signed listener failed its stage fetch no matter what the operator selected (and a proxy-only environment could never be reached). The wiring is now a pure, tested mapping (`stagerFetch`), and the generated stager builds a real TLS configuration — optional system-root replacement via the CA bundle, optional client certificate, then the proxy — instead of starting from an empty transport
- Certificate uploads and regeneration honour a configured `server.key_file`: both handlers read it and then unconditionally replaced the value with a `server.key` sibling derived from the certificate path, so the new certificate was paired with whatever key still lived at the configured location — and `GenerateSelfSignedCert` never created the key's directory when the two live apart. The configured path wins now (a sibling key is derived only when unset), the directory creation is checked, and regression tests cover both the upload and the regenerate path
- A workflow's P2 jump guard actually bounds execution: `jumpBudget` was initialized inside the per-step loop, so it was reset on every iteration and the bound was a no-op — a step whose branch target was itself kept creating tasks forever. The budget now lives per agent for the whole run, and `workflow_engine_jump_test.go` pins a self-jumping workflow to stopping at the configured ceiling
- The Linux credential dump no longer reads SSH keys and discards them: the section built a `name (N bytes)` + contents description, but the output line printed only the key path and the description was never used (dead store), so key paths came back with no material while the darwin agent returns the contents. The linux section now emits `[+] path (N bytes)` followed by the file contents for files under 10 KB, matching darwin
- Sleep masking in the Go agent finally runs: `InitSleepMask()` executed in `init()` before `applyAdaptiveEvasion()` — the only place that can set `enableSleepMask` from the detected EDR strategy — so the mask never activated on any platform and `sleepWithMask` was never reached; the `set_sleep_mask` task also switched the active mask without turning masked sleeping on. The strategy decision now runs after the EDR apply, an explicit operator switch activates the mask (and initializes the platform buffer even when the strategy did not opt in), and the mask registry offers only the two real masks (`config-xor`, `xor`) under honest names
- The weekly secrets scan is green again: the scheduled `secrets` job had failed since 2026-09-28 on 221 findings that a full-history scan attributes to generated or removed material, none present on HEAD (a scan of the current tree reports zero). Generated UI bundles under `internal/webdist/dist/` (154) and the vendored `vis-network.min.js` (1) are allowlisted by path, as are the removed server-template and Next.js trees (17 + 2); 41 unit-test fixtures (byte-ramp keys such as `aabbccdd…` / `0123456789abcdef…`, explicit `test-jwt-secret…` names) and 4 non-secret strings (localStorage keys, a `s.cfg.Crypto.ExtC2Key` field access) are allowlisted by exact value pattern. Two historical real-looking values — `config_blob.go`'s static config-blob builder key and `setup-dev.sh`'s pre-v2.4 XOR default, both absent from main — are ignored per commit in `.gitleaksignore` instead of by value, so re-introducing either is still reported (the builder key should be treated as compromised: it was published in this repository's history). Verified with the CI's gitleaks 8.24.3: 221 → 0 findings over the full history, using the action's command shape (`--redact --exit-code=2`, SARIF), with config and ignore-file auto-discovery

### Changed

- Every panel in the console now wears the same header (2026-10 UI/UX batch U8 — the card system). Four header implementations had drifted apart: a bare 24px icon with an inline description (`SectionCard`), a 36px icon tile with a stacked description (`CardHeaderRow`), a hand-rolled row with a coloured square (`CollectCard`, `ChartCard`, `ConfigSection`) and a raw `CardHeader` grid. They collapse into one primitive, `components/ui/card-header.tsx`, and every one of those components renders it: a muted icon tile carrying a hue-coloured glyph, the title with an optional count badge, an optional two-line description, and right-aligned controls. The remaining hand-written card titles on the Attack, DNS, Infrastructure, Macros, Campaign, Proxy Chains, Listener detail and beacon-detail pages were converted, so a card reads the same whether it holds a chart, a table, a form or a collect result. The header is flush with the card edge (the card's own padding and gap are neutralised) instead of floating below a white strip with a second 40px gap under it, and the section title is a real heading instead of a `div` in one family and an `h3` in another. The login card and the payload-generator side panel keep their deliberately different shapes.
- List pages keep their filters on screen. The Audit Log, Plugins, Token Store and the Events → Alerts tab move their filter rows into the shared sticky `PageToolbar` that Beacons, Listeners, Loot and the task list already used, so the controls stay reachable while a long table scrolls; the toolbar pins directly under the top bar. Embedded panels opt out of stickiness, because the Events page's own tab rail is already sticky and a second sticky layer slid underneath it (measured: 36px of overlap).
- Filter dropdowns finally say what they mean. Selects that used a sentinel value (`all`, `none`, `placeholder`, `__all`) rendered that raw token in the trigger, because the item's label could not be resolved until the menu mounted — the Audit page showed two selects reading "all", the Traffic Log showed "__all", the BloodHound and privilege-escalation agent pickers showed "placeholder", the NTLM relay page showed "none", and the payload generator's build options showed their internal keys. All of them now render a localized label through the select's value renderer, matching the pattern the credential vault already used.
- Empty states stop eating the page. The shared empty state reserved ~240px (a 56px icon plate over `min-h-40` plus `md:py-20`), which turned every empty panel on the dashboard and in a card body into a wall of whitespace; it now reserves ~130px with a 44px plate, and the campaign table's own 80px cell padding was brought in line with the rest.
- The console has one page rhythm (2026-10 UI/UX batch U9, wave 1 — the layout shell). The shell's padding was four hard-coded Tailwind steps and the page blocks were spaced by four different `space-y-*` ramps, so the same screen could carry 16, 20 and 24 px gaps depending on the page. Three tokens now own the geometry: `--page-pad-x` / `--page-pad-y` for the shell's outer padding and `--page-gap` for the space between a page's blocks, stepping at 640 px and 1024 px exactly like the steps they replaced (measured page widths are unchanged: 358 / 720 / 728 / 1144 px at 390 / 768 / 1024 / 1440). The shell also stopped clamping the content width a second time — the inner `PageContainer` owns `standard` (80rem), `wide` (96rem) and `workspace`, so a narrow page is no longer fought over by two nested max-widths. The 403 surface no longer renders a `<main>` inside the shell's `<main>`, and its `min-h-screen` overflowed the viewport by exactly one top bar.
- Filter rows now have one sticky implementation, and it actually sticks. `position: sticky` is bounded by the top edge of its containing block, so a filter row that was the first child of the page body had a travel budget of zero and could never pin, however far the page scrolled; the Dashboard's view switcher had no sticky behaviour at all and scrolled away with the panels below it. The `PageContainer` toolbar slot is now the single home of the page-level filter row and pins under the top bar by default (`stickyToolbar`, with an opt-out for the panels embedded in the Events tab rail, whose own rail is already sticky); the dead `stickyHeader` and `aside` props are gone. Measured after the change: the Dashboard row pins at 60 px, the Audit and Plugins rows at 56 px, and no route reports a filter row that scrolls away while content remains below it.
- Cards have one interior padding (2026-10 UI/UX batch U9, wave 2 — the card body). The card primitive already derives its padding from `--card-spacing` (16 px, 20 px from 640 px), but 62 call sites overrode it with a hard-coded step — `p-3`, `p-4`, `p-5`, `p-6`, `p-3.5`, `p-3 sm:p-4` — so the same panel had a 12, 16, 20 or 24 px interior depending on the page, and `SectionCard` (which renders its body unpadded) pushed that decision onto every consumer. All of them now read `p-(--card-spacing)`; the flush cards that hold a table keep their edge-to-edge `p-0`. The dashboard's five hand-copied list rows became one `ListRow` primitive (leading slot, two-line text block, right slot) on the shared hover surface, so a panel that looks like a list no longer has to be restyled by hand.
- Wide tables keep the operator's actions on screen (2026-10 UI/UX batch U9, wave 3 — the table body). A table wider than its container scrolls sideways, and the last column — the one with the buttons — went with it: the Listeners page clipped "Copy / Edit / Stop / Delete" by 144 px on a 1440 px screen, and below 1024 px the action column was gone entirely. The action column now pins (`sticky-end`): the cell stays glued to the right edge and paints the row's own surface (stripe, hover) so the columns sliding underneath never show through it. Secondary columns step out with the viewport instead of only below 640 px — Listeners drops type, tags and the beacon count below 1024 px, the address below 768 px and the notes below 1536 px; the Beacons table's 850 px floor became 640 px until 1280 px; Circuit Breaker, Campaign and OPSEC each drop one column on phones. The layout audit now treats an overflowing table as a violation unless one of its columns is pinned, and the run is green: 45 routes × 4 viewports, zero violations.
- The three CI gates that were red on `main` are green again (2026-10): the OpenAPI spec now documents the eight routes the checker was missing (the file scan / search / thumbnail endpoints, the WebRTC start / stop pair and the two remote-desktop signalling sockets — coverage back to 677/677, 100 %), the lint debt the Authenticode batch left behind is paid down (the dead Cobalt Strike v1 parser and the unused `malleableParseCSFallback` are gone, the ELF validator's ineffectual assignment is a declared variable, the two capitalized Authenticode error strings are lower-cased — both targets are down to the pure gosec backlog, 67 and 68, and the ceiling follows), and the toolchain moves to Go 1.26.10 with `pion/stun` 3.1.6, which clears the two `govulncheck` findings (`crypto/x509` GO-2026-6809 and `pion/stun` GO-2026-6163).
- Dialogs have one width scale and keep their title and actions on screen (2026-10 UI/UX batch U9, wave 4 — the dialog body). 72 `DialogContent` instances spread over nine widths (`sm` 4, `md` 34, `lg` 14, `xl` 3, `2xl` 8, `3xl` 4, `4xl` 2, `5xl` 2) plus one raw `w-[95vw]`, each written as a `max-w-*` class inside the call site; the footer's negative margins were hard-wired to the popup's `p-5`, so the band and the padding could only be kept in step by hand. A dialog now declares its size (`sm` / `md` / `lg` / `xl` / `2xl` / `full`) and the widths live in one table in the primitive; the popup's padding is the token `--dialog-padding` and the footer band cancels it with the same token, resolved opaque so a pinned band never lets the form show through. The header and the footer pin while a long form scrolls under them, so the title and the action buttons stay reachable, and the side panels got the same size scale.
- The embedded bundle is finally the same bundle everywhere (2026-10). The bundle built on a Windows workstation could never match the CI's Linux build — 424 diffs, i.e. every file in the export. Two causes, one hiding the other: `lightningcss` downgraded the `oklch()` palette to `lab()` for browsers that only speak `lab` and printed the converted numbers with a platform-dependent precision (36.4219 on Windows, 36.422 on Linux), and one byte changed the CSS file's hash, the name of every chunk importing it and the name of every HTML page referencing it; and two modules shared the basename `TrafficPage` (the traffic log and the beacon's traffic tab), so the bundler disambiguated their chunk names from the module path, which the two platforms spell differently. The CSS target is now Safari 16, which speaks `oklch()` natively — no downgrade, no precision drift, and 25 kB less CSS — and the beacon detail page is `BeaconTrafficPage` like its siblings. A Linux export of a commit and a Windows build of it now agree on all 346 file names and on every byte. A dispatch-only `webdist-export` workflow renders the bundle on ubuntu and uploads it as an artifact, so the committed bundle can be refreshed from a Linux host the same way the Linux visual baselines are.
- Form fields have one structure (2026-10 UI/UX batch U9, wave 5 — the form field). The 373 labels in the console spelled their own typography at every call site — ten different class combinations and four different label-to-control gaps — and the error text was usually not wired to its control with `aria-describedby`. The label styles are now owned by the `Label` primitive alone: 233 call sites dropped their repeated `text-xs` / `font-medium` / `text-muted-foreground` and the two gaps collapsed into one. A new `Field` primitive owns the whole field — label, control, hint and error, one spacing, the required marker and the error's `aria-describedby` — and the credential and user dialogs are the reference implementations.

- The product speaks one professional vocabulary and gives every page an emoji identity (2026-10 UI/UX batch U7, wave 1 — the naming program's copy layer): chrome that mixed Session / Agent / Implant now says **Beacon** everywhere (45 navigation labels, their 42 matching page titles, and the dashboard tiles — Sessions → Beacons, "No sessions" → "No beacons"), and the surrounding terminology is sharpened the way a C2 operator says it: Generate → **Payload Generator**, PrivEsc → **Privilege Escalation**, Macros → **Task Macros**, Exploit Toolkit → **Post-Exploitation Toolkit**, Network Map → **Network Topology**, Chain → **Proxy Chains**, Cloud → **Cloud Credential Theft**, NTLM → **NTLM Relay & Coercion**, User Mgmt → **User Management**, Asset organization → **Asset Management**, Agent Groups → **Beacon Groups**, Campaign → **Campaigns**, Scanner → **Network Scanner**, Traffic → **Traffic Log**, Global Token Vault → **Token Store**, Screenshot Collection → **Loot**, BOF Management → **BOF Library**, Security Audit Logs → **Audit Log**, Integration Hub → **Integrations**, Pivoting & Tunnels → **Pivoting**, Attack → **MITRE ATT&CK Coverage**. The ZH side mirrors all of it and repairs long-standing mistranslations (链式攻击 → 代理链, 横向穿透 → 跳板, 截图收集 → 战利品, 事件流 → 事件, 熔断器 → 断路器, 全局令牌库 → 令牌仓库). Each page then carries exactly one emoji, used identically in the sidebar label, the page h1, the breadcrumb and `document.title` (🐝 Beacons, 📡 Listeners, 🔔 Events, 📦 Payload Generator, 🧰 Post-Exploitation Toolkit …) — decoration only, `aria-hidden` inside chips and never the sole carrier of meaning; 92 i18n values change per language and the 4727-key parity set is untouched. The U6 residue closes in the same snapshot pass: the DNS stat tile loses its stray `tone="primary"` (the listeners' six-tile grid reads as one family), `CardHeaderRow` titles clamp to two lines instead of a one-line ellipsis (the traffic page's "Beacon Communication" header no longer cuts off on a phone), the privesc check-type chips turn eleven `"??"`/`"???"` mojibake literals into real emoji and clamp their descriptions to two lines instead of truncating ("Run all available privilege es…"), and the dashboard geo legend falls back to 📍 instead of `"??"`. The terminology contract — vocabulary, nav-equals-title rule, emoji catalog, and the L1/L2/L3 scope split — is written down in `docs/TERMINOLOGY.md`. Verified: unit 883/172, `npm run check` 11/11 (4727 keys, bundle 3.99 MB / critical 970 kB unchanged), the full Playwright suite 156 passed / 36 skipped, and all 28 win32 visual baselines regenerated (nav labels and h1 emoji repaint every surface; the linux set follows via `visual-baselines.yml`). Route URLs, page directories and the backend keep their names in this wave — wave 2 renames the six operator-facing routes with redirects, and the backend keeps saying `agents` by contract.
- The URL bar now speaks the same vocabulary as the UI (2026-10 UI/UX batch U7, wave 2 — the naming program's routing layer): six sections move to their professional names — `/agents` → **`/beacons`**, `/timeline` → **`/events`**, `/generate` → **`/payload-generator`**, `/privesc` → **`/privilege-escalation`**, `/chain` → **`/proxy-chains`**, `/asset-organization` → **`/asset-management`** — and the whole `/agents/:id/…` detail family (config, files, persistence, remote-desktop, screen, shell, token, traffic) follows its list page to `/beacons/:id/…`, so the address bar stops contradicting the sidebar. Every pre-rename URL stays addressable instead of 404-ing: the static export still ships each old route's shell (`vite-plugins/staticExport.ts` lists them beside the new ones and the lockstep test pins both directions), the router rewrites them client-side through one shared `LegacyPrefixRedirect` that swaps only the first path segment and carries the id, sub-route, query string and hash across the hop — a bookmarked `/agents?status=offline` lands on `/beacons?status=offline`, `/agents/<id>/shell` on `/beacons/<id>/shell` — and the redirects are registered as ordinary routes, so they are permission-mapped and covered by the same table tests as every other page. No backend change was needed: the static middleware answers browser navigations before gin's router, and the legacy per-page handlers (Next.js-era JSON renderers) were already unreachable for them; the HTTP API, sqlite schema, OpenAPI document and the dual-use `/agents/:id/…` API paths are untouched, as the program's L3 boundary requires. The page directories and their route-entry components follow the URLs (`pages/agents` → `pages/beacons` + `BeaconsPageContent`, `pages/timeline` → `pages/events`, `pages/generate` → `pages/payload-generator` + `PayloadGeneratorPage`, `pages/privesc` → `pages/privilege-escalation` + `PrivilegeEscalationPage`, `pages/chain` → `pages/proxy-chains` + `ProxyChainsPage`, `pages/asset-organization` → `pages/asset-management` + `AssetManagementPage`), 175 files moved with `git mv`; identifiers that mirror the backend contract deliberately keep saying "agent" (`agentId`, `lib/api-paths.ts`, the detail sub-components, `AgentHeader`/`AgentStatusBar`/…) — they are the data model, not the label. Visual baselines were renamed, not regenerated: `agents-*` → `beacons-*` and `timeline-*` → `events-*` on both platforms, byte-identical because not a pixel moved, and the parity check still sees 28 snapshot names per platform. The static e2e suite gains seven legacy-redirect cases that assert the canonical URL *and* the surviving query string, and the six renamed routes replace their old slugs in the shell-boot and layout route lists. Adding the shells grew the committed bundle by 30 HTML files (307 → 337); the JS/CSS budgets are unmoved (3.99 MB total, 971 kB critical path, 190 files).
- The body copy catches up with the vocabulary (2026-10 UI/UX batch U7, wave 3 — the naming program's copy layer, first page domain): the Beacons surface — list, eight detail tabs, interact dock and the shared status banner — stops switching dialects mid-sentence. An inventory over both language blocks flagged 100 values (`agents.*` 97 + `status_banner.*` 3); 87 are rewritten per language (170 value edits, keys untouched, per the program's immutable-key rule) and 13 are deliberately kept with recorded reasons. The rules the sweep settled, now written into `docs/TERMINOLOGY.md`: a *running* implant is a **beacon** ("Waiting for the implant to return this result" → "Waiting for the beacon to return this result", "Agent session" → "Beacon", "该 Agent 未上报…" → "该 Beacon 未上报…"); the *generated artifact* is a **payload** ("Generate Implant" → **Generate Payload**, "Deploy your first implant" → "Deploy your first payload"); the check-in *event* is a **check-in** in English and 回连 in Chinese ("Agent will exit on next beacon" → "The beacon will exit on its next check-in"); and "Session" survives only where it is a real word — the `User-Agent` HTTP header family (3 keys), the AMSI-session technique name, the recon category for host logon/network sessions, the Windows logon-session token source, the RDP/remote-desktop copy, the console-session-lifetime strings in the media panel, and the WeChat pair where 会话 means *chat*. The invariant is that whatever the flag was, the *entity* a sentence is about decides the noun, not the key's history. Two e2e locators follow their copy (the beacons search placeholder and the row checkbox's accessible name), and the visual-baseline refresh is scoped rather than wholesale: of the fourteen files the two `--update-snapshots=all` passes rewrote between them, only the four `beacons-*` ones are adopted — the toolbar button change (Generate Implant → Generate Payload) was confirmed by cropping the regeneration against the committed baseline — while the other ten (ai/dashboard/events/listeners/login) were restored, because the status banner returns `null` under the mocked stats, no rewritten key renders on those pages, and the two passes did not even rewrite the same set (two of the rejected files regenerated byte-identical on the second pass). Gates: eslint, `tsc --noEmit`, 172 files / 883 unit tests, the eleven-member `npm run check` chain (i18n parity at 4727 keys, bundle 3.99 MB / 190 files, critical path 972 kB), and the full e2e suite at 184 passed / 36 skipped; the linux baselines land through the dispatch-only `visual-baselines.yml` with the same scoping — 28 files rendered from this commit, 21 byte-identical to the committed set, the four `beacons-*` files adopted (the copy-driven diff measures 1.401 % of the dark-laptop canvas) and three rejected as sub-tolerance jitter (11 px on `ai-light-laptop`, 0.445 % and 0.109 % on the two `listeners-*-mobile` shots)
- The copy sweep reaches the payload pipeline (2026-10 UI/UX batch U7, wave 3 — second page domain): the payload generator, the build-record page and the profiles page now say what the rest of the console says. A domain inventory flags 33 values (14 `generate.*`, 10 `profiles.*`, 5 `builds.*`, 4 `oneshot.*`); 24 are rewritten per language (46 value edits, keys untouched) and 5 are kept with recorded reasons (`HTTP Proxy` / HTTP 代理, the two `User-Agent` labels, and the settings-reload warning's "new sessions", which are login sessions). The format grid stops calling itself "Agent Binaries" — it offers six *formats* (exe, dll, ps1, ELF, Mach-O, one-liner), so it is **Payload Formats** / 载荷格式, and the e2e smoke case that names it follows. The generated artifact is a payload wherever it is named ("Implant build records" → **Payload build records**, "Generate a new implant to get started" → "Generate a new payload…", "Generated agents connect back to C2 automatically" → "…payloads…", "Agents already generated with it are unaffected" → "Payloads…"), and the running thing is a beacon ("The implant would dial a port…" → "The beacon would dial…", "Agent modules" → **Beacon modules**, "Agent Version Distribution" → **Beacon Version Distribution** with its unit "{n} agents" → "{n} beacons", "Push to Agent" → **Push to Beacon**, "Load agents" → **Load beacons** — the Chinese button had said 加载主机, hosts, and now says 加载 Beacon). Two rules join `docs/TERMINOLOGY.md`: a *format descriptor* names the format, not the entity ("GUI implant" → **GUI executable**, beside `sideload` / `script one-liner` / `ELF · amd64/arm`), and where the sentence is about the code or the build, a subjectless clause beats reintroducing the banned noun ("After implant changes, regenerate the payload" → "Regenerate the payload after code changes"; "Not available on macOS implants" → "…in macOS builds"). The four `oneshot.*` values are deferred with a reason — they are the one-shot *scheduled task* card on the automation page, not a payload one-shot. No baseline moves: none of the three pages is a visual route (`VISUAL_ROUTES` is login, dashboard, beacons, listeners, events, settings, ai), so both the win32 and the linux sets pass unchanged. Gates: eslint and `tsc --noEmit` clean, 172 files / 883 unit tests, `npm run check` 11/11 (parity 4727, bundle 3.99 MB / 190 files, critical path 972 kB), the full e2e suite at 184 passed / 36 skipped, with the `out/` export rebuilt and the 337-file embedded `dist` re-synced before the suite ran
- The copy sweep takes the automation page (2026-10 UI/UX batch U7, wave 3 — third page domain): the triggers/alert rules, the scheduled-tasks card, the one-shot card and the workflow workspace now use the console's vocabulary too. The inventory over the automation cluster flags 27 values across six namespaces (`auto.*` 6, `scheduler.*` 4, `oneshot.*` 4, `workflows.*` 5, plus `macros.*` 5 and `autotag.*` 3 that belong to their own pages); this wave rewrites 19 per language (38 value edits, keys untouched), keeps **none** — the cluster carries no legitimate session or user-agent copy — and defers 8 with reasons (the macros page and the auto-tag rules page keep their own domain). The event and alert-rule labels follow the entity ("Agent offline" / "Agent online" → **Beacon offline** / **Beacon online**, in both the trigger list and the rule-type select, while the wire enum ids `agent_offline`, `agent_online` and `agent.disconnect` stay untouched — they are contract, not copy). The pickers stop calling the target an agent ("Select agent" → **Select beacon** / 选择 Beacon; the ZH scheduled card had said 代理 and the list warning 代理列表), the four `oneshot.*` strings wave 2 deferred are swept here, and the workflow scope reads beacons outright ("All Agents" → **All Beacons**, "Selected Agents" → **Selected Beacons**, the scope-id placeholder "…or agent ids" → "…or beacon ids") down to its dispatch toast ("Executed: {task_count} tasks to {agents_count} **beacons**"). No visual route is involved, so no baseline moves; and no e2e or unit locator depended on these strings (the pre-existing `agent offline` literals in the unit suites are mock rejection messages, not copy). Gates: eslint and `tsc --noEmit` clean, 172 files / 883 unit tests, `npm run check` 11/11 (parity 4727, bundle 3.99 MB / 190 files, critical path 972 kB), the full e2e suite at 184 passed / 36 skipped
- The Beacons cluster is actually complete now (2026-10 UI/UX batch U7, wave 3 — the residue pass): the sweep had been scoped by *key prefix*, so `shell.*`, `cred.*`, `screen.*` and `hostinfo.*` — the shell console, the screen viewer and the credential/host-info surfaces the beacon pages render — were never inventoried, and the wave-3 deployment check caught one of them live ("Agent 离线" on the host-info card). All four namespaces are swept here: 28 flagged values, 21 rewritten per language (39 value edits), 7 kept with reasons, 0 deferred. The kept seven are the cookie-proxy family (`Cookie proxy failed` / Cookie 代理失败 …, the whole status/start/stop cluster), which is a real local HTTP proxy rather than an implant synonym. The rewritten ones settle the shell console's vocabulary: its tabs are keyed by beacon id (`closeTab(tab.agentId)`), so "Shell sessions" → **Beacon shells**, "New session tab" → **New shell tab**, "Close session" → **Close tab**, "Switch session" → **Switch beacon** (the select calls `openAgentTab`), "Back to agent" → **Back to beacon**, and the status/offline/streaming strings follow ("Agent is {status}" → **Beacon is {status}**, "…still running on the agent" → "…on the beacon", "waiting for implant output…" → "waiting for beacon output…", with the ZH bare `implant` loan words retiring). The credential surfaces follow too ("Target session" → **Target beacon**, "Harvest from session" → **Harvest from beacon**, "Select a session first" → "Select a beacon first", "…auto-parsed from agent tasks" → "…from beacon tasks", "{skipped} entries skipped (no password/agent or fuse)" → "…(no password/beacon or fuse)", and the harvest explainer's "implant tasks" → "beacon tasks"), as do the screen viewer ("Waiting for the agent to return a fresh frame…" → "…the beacon…") and the host-info card. Gates: eslint and `tsc --noEmit` clean, 172 files / 883 unit tests, `npm run check` 11/11 (parity 4727, bundle 3.99 MB / 190 files, critical path 972 kB), the full e2e suite at 184 passed / 36 skipped — the beacon list's visual baseline does not move, because the only `cred.*` keys that page renders (`quality_scripted`, `missing_module`, `module_status_unknown`) are unflagged and untouched
- The listeners page follows the vocabulary (2026-10 UI/UX batch U7, wave 3 — fourth page domain): the list page and the listener detail now speak of beacons and payloads instead of agents and implants. The inventory ran the method fix first — every namespace `pages/listeners/**` renders (`cb.*`, `dashboard.*`, `generate.*`, `listener.*`, `listeners.*`, `status.*`) — which surfaced the *singular* `listener.*` namespace a prefix-scoped pass would have missed: 12 rewritten per language (24 value edits), 0 kept, 2 deferred with reasons (`cb.honesty_desc` belongs to the circuit-breaker page; `dashboard.quick_generate` renders on the dashboard, a visual route whose wave carries the baseline work). The detail page's stats and headings follow the rows they count ("Associated Agent Total" → **Associated Beacon Total**, "Agents Using This Listener" → **Beacons Using This Listener**, "View All Agents" → **View All Beacons**), its empty state stops calling the client an implant ("No Implant uses this listener" → **"No beacons use this listener"**), and its action follows rule 7 ("Generate Implant for this Listener" → **"Generate Payload for this Listener"**, and it does link to the payload generator). The list page's column headers and count warnings follow ("Agent" → **Beacon**, "Failed to load listener agent counts" → "…beacon counts"), as do the hero subtitle ("…to receive implant connections … when generating implants" → "…to receive beacon connections … when generating a payload"), the tip ("…connect agents to the corresponding address" → "…connect beacons…") and the load-balancing hint ("Agents can randomly or by policy connect" → "Beacons can…"). Gates: eslint and `tsc --noEmit` clean, 172 files / 883 unit tests, `npm run check` 11/11 (parity 4727, bundle 3.99 MB / 190 files, critical path 972 kB), the full e2e suite at 184 passed / 36 skipped. Both visual routes stay within contract: the copy moves fewer than 1.5 % of pixels on the win32 set (no baseline refresh), and the linux set was re-rendered from this commit and compared — the numbers are in `docs/UIUX_AUDIT.md`
- The macros and auto-tag pages follow (2026-10 UI/UX batch U7, wave 3 — fifth page domain): the macro library and its run dialog, and the auto-tag rules page, now use the console's vocabulary. Nine values flag (macros `agents_unreadable` / `empty_message` / `no_agents` / `select_agents_hint` / `toast.dispatched`, autotag `applied` / `empty_message` / `subtitle`, and `ai.pb_agent` — the playbook builder's target selector renders on the macros page as well as the AI page, so it moves with this wave while the rest of `ai.*` stays for the AI domain); all nine are rewritten (17 value edits — `macros.empty_message`'s ZH already said Beacon, so it is an EN-only edit), 0 kept, 0 deferred. The macro flow reads "No agents available" → **"No beacons available"**, "Select the agents to run this macro on. Each agent executes the steps independently and sequentially." → "Select the **beacons**… Each **beacon**…", "Macro dispatched to {count} agents" → "…to {count} **beacons**", "Record a command sequence and replay it on any agent with one click." → "…on any **beacon**…", and the unread-list warning ("The agent list could not be read…" → "The **beacon** list…"). The auto-tag page follows ("Applied auto-tags to {count} agent(s)" → "…to {count} **beacon**(s)", "Create one to automatically tag agents on check-in." → "…tag **beacons** on check-in.", and the section subtitle drops 代理 for Beacon), with the ZH side retiring 签入 for 回连 per rule 5, and the playbook selector reads "Personalize for beacon (optional)" / 针对 Beacon 定制. - The users page keeps its sessions and drops its agent wording (2026-10 UI/UX batch U7, wave 3 — sixth page domain): the page is the cleanest "keeps" case in the whole sweep.
