- The settings tree's error surfaces close out (2026-10 UI/UX batch U3 follow-up, wave 15 — the family that opened the batch, closing its residue): the eight list-bearing sections behind Administration retire their nine raw-message reads, which wave 1 left behind because they were display states rather than toasts. The five single-surface loads — API keys, ExtC2 channels, notification routes, notifications and SIEM rules — resolve their unread warnings through `errorText`: mapped status copy first (401/403/429/5xx), backend text for other 4xx, the network copy for a browser `TypeError`, and only a message-less failure keeps the section's existing fallback key. The old code rendered `Failed to fetch` verbatim and, for an empty message, an empty string — which, being falsy, painted the *empty state* ("nothing configured") over an unread list. The three dual-surface sites resolve once and reuse the string for both surfaces, so the warning and its toast cannot disagree: `BackupSection`'s unread warning plus load toast, `ModulesSection`'s module list plus toast, and `useSettingsData`'s error state plus toast; `ModulesSection`'s agent-list banner (the deploy selector's unread-list warning) follows the same rule. `ReloadStatusCard`'s status probe stops swallowing the failure reason: the toast routes through `toastError` under the existing `settings.reload_status_failed` fallback while the card body keeps that copy for an unread matrix, so a dead backend is distinguishable from a failed poll. Verified and deliberately kept: `EmergencySection`'s kill-switch status and `useTOTP`'s 2FA status hold a translated presence flag whose string is never rendered (the UI gates on truthiness and renders its fixed unknown-state copy), so they are flags, not message surfaces; `SettingsPage`'s hash parse and localStorage reads stay silent by design and validation toasts stay direct. Counts: `useErrorToast` importing files 54 → 55; the touched files' raw `set*Error` reads 9 → 0 (grep-verified — the whole of the settings tree's remaining tail); repo-wide, the `x.message`-ternary tail drops 53 → 44 (wave 14's ledger minus exactly the nine settings sites); no new keys (4727 used keys unchanged). Coverage: new `loadErrors.test.tsx` (13 — a five-section table pinning the 5xx mapping and the message-less fallback, plus three `ModulesSection` cases: the dual-surface resolve-once, the agent-list banner, and the message-less fallback on both surfaces), new `BackupSection.test.tsx` (2) and `ReloadStatusCard.test.tsx` (2 — the probe reason plus a success-render control), and `useSettingsData.test.ts` 4 → 8 (5xx / network / message-less / rate-limited, asserted on both surfaces); 20 of the 21 new tests verified red against the pre-migration code, and the four existing `useSettingsData` pins (raw-text passthrough) stay green both ways.
- The agent files workspace's error surfaces close out (2026-10 UI/UX batch U3 follow-up, wave 14): `useAgentFiles` — the hook behind both `/agents/:id/files` and the dock's file panel — retires its 17 raw failure sinks, the largest single-file cluster on the U3 map: 15 `String(err)` coercion sites (which rendered `Error: …` prefixes or coerced plain rejection objects) plus the two `.message` ternaries behind the listing retry card and the OS-detection blocker. Twelve un-prefixed toasts route through `toastError` (listing, exfil, preview read, delete, mkdir, rename, chmod, upload push, drives, USB, find/hunt), the three batch fan-outs (batch delete's dispatch and poll phases, batch pull) resolve through `errorText` under their filename prefix, and both displayed states resolve through the same rule with their toasts' fallback keys, so a toast and its persistent card cannot disagree. The drive surface gains the `agents.files_drives_failed` key (EN + ZH; the operation previously had no failure copy). Behavior: a status-carrying rejection shows the mapped status copy (401/403/429/5xx), a backend 4xx its own text, and the upload pump's rejected `AbortError` is silent. Since the dock's file panel was already normalized and this hook powers both surfaces, they now share one key set and one semantics. Counts: `useErrorToast` importing files 53 → 54; the touched file's raw sinks 17 → 0 and repo-wide `String(err)` sites 30 → 15 (grep-verified); the repo-wide `x.message`-ternary tail 56 → 53 (2 toast-level, 21 inline, 30 helper); one new key (4727 used keys). Coverage: new `useAgentFiles.errors.test.ts` (8) — mapped 5xx/429 for the toast and the card, backend-4xx passthrough, the message-less fallback, the verbatim local message, prefixed batch failures, the TypeError→network mapping and abort silence; all 8 verified red against the pre-migration code, with the three existing suites green both ways as controls.
- The agent console's error surfaces close out (2026-10 UI/UX batch U3 follow-up, wave 13): the deliberate 409-approval-warning trio and the cluster's five inline raw-message reads now share one mechanism. `useErrorToast` gains `warnError`, the warning-toned twin of `toastError` (same normalization and message rules — mapped status copy, backend text for outcome-specific 4xx, fallback key only for a message-less failure — with aborted requests silent in both), and the three 409 branches — `AISuggestCard`'s run command, `useAgentQuickShell`'s quick shell, `AgentDetailPage`'s header quick-action — route through it, so an empty approval notice falls back to the surface's copy instead of toasting an empty string; the quick-shell history row resolves the same conflict reason through `errorText` with the toast's fallback key, so the row and the warning cannot disagree. `HostInfoCard` resolves its sweep failure through `errorText` — the cluster's one *displayed* string, and a plain rejection object used to render the generic copy for every failure, so a 5xx now shows the mapped status copy and a backend 4xx its own text. The display-inert raw reads retire through the same resolver: `TimelineSection`'s timeline load, `TokenPage`'s token-list and process-list loads, and `useAgentData`'s lock table (whose plain-rejection form used to store `[object Object]`), each state keeping only its presence-flag role. Counts: `useErrorToast` importing files 52 → 53; the 8 touched files' error-surface `.message` reads 9 → 0 (grep-verified); the repo-wide `x.message`-ternary tail 65 → 56 (3 toast-level, 23 inline, 30 helper); no new keys (4726 used keys unchanged). - The tasks and macros workspaces' error surfaces close out (2026-10 UI/UX batch U3 follow-up, wave 12): the cluster's 13 failure sinks across five files now route through the normalizer (11 raw-message ternaries plus two reason-swallowing catches), so a status-carrying rejection shows the mapped status copy (401/403/429/5xx) or the backend's own message (other 4xx) instead of untranslated text. `TasksPageContent`'s task-list load resolves once through `errorText` for both the inline `DataError` and the toast, and the local `mutationError` helper behind its six task actions (cancel/rerun/approve/reject/claim/release) now goes through the same rule — a rejected cancel surfaces the backend's 409/404 text or the mapped status copy — while the two bulk fan-outs keep their deliberate per-item failure counting. `AIAnalysisPanel`, `NLQueryDialog` and `AIPlaybookDialog` route their POST rejections through `toastError` (their success-but-empty-payload toasts stay direct: not thrown errors). `MacrosPageContent` normalizes its three load failures through `errorText` with the existing `common.load_failed` fallback — the inline "library/runs/agents unreadable" banners keep their `{message}` template — and its save/run/delete/stop failures through `toastError`; delete and stop previously swallowed the reason whole, so a rejected delete now reports the backend's reason instead of the generic copy. Test note: the two dialog suites wrap their trigger clicks in `act` so the already-rejected request's continuation (and its loading-state update) settles inside an act window, which keeps stderr free of React act warnings; the page suites polyfill `matchMedia` and `Element.prototype.scrollTo` (jsdom ships neither).
- The `/ai` conversation workspace's error surfaces close out (2026-10 UI/UX batch U3 follow-up, wave 11): the cluster's 18 raw-message sinks across six files now route through the normalizer, so a status-carrying rejection shows the mapped status copy (401/403/429/5xx) or the backend's own message (other 4xx) instead of untranslated text. `AIPageContent` normalizes six action surfaces — session rename and creation (both previously swallowed the reason whole), the fire-and-forget run-cancel (`.catch(() => ...)` becomes `.catch((e) => toastError(e, ...))`), branch creation, pin and archive — so a rejected pin surfaces the reason under `ai.pin_session_failed` instead of the raw string. `AIAssistBar` routes the run-review rejection through `toastError` and normalizes the macro-load inline error through `errorText` (display-inert today — the picker only disables — but the raw read retires). `AIContextPanel` resolves its load failure once and re-uses the string for both the inline banner and the toast, and normalizes the upload / remove / policy-update toasts (the policy toggle still rolls back before reporting). `PendingAIIntents` normalizes the approve/reject failure. `useAIConfig` and `useAISessions` share the two-surface split: the config and session-list loads resolve once through `errorText` for the inline `configError` / `sessionsError` banner and re-use that string for the toast, while save, message-load and delete go through `toastError`. - The `formatThrownError` tail closes (2026-10 UI/UX batch U3 follow-up, wave 10 — the last action-surface users of the helper): every page that still rendered a status-carrying rejection as `[object Object]` now routes through the normalizer. `NtlmPage` turns coercion, relay-start and relay-stop into three mutations behind one derived pending (the OR of the three `isPending`s — the retired shared bool's only job — so a second request cannot start mid-flight), keeps its `data.success` else-branches (a success-less envelope is a real shape: `unwrapBody` unwraps only `success === true` bodies, so the branch is not provably dead), and a rejected coerce now reports the backend's own message instead of `[object Object]`. `PivotingPageContent` routes the direct-SOCKS start rejection through the normalizer under the new `pivoting.toast.direct_socks_failed` fallback, while the per-row busy identity stays hand-rolled in `try/finally` — rows can fire in parallel and a single mutation would drop a sibling completion (the wave-9 dock-files rule). `usePivotingData`'s six failure paths — five of which swallowed the reason whole (`catch { toast.error(t(...)) }`) — now report through the normalizer, so a rejected relay, local proxy or RPort action surfaces the backend's reason or the mapped status copy (new `pivoting.toast.rport_start_failed`; the other five keys stay). `ToolkitPage` normalizes the quick-action dispatch and the template `onRun` path (new `templates.toast.run_failed`) while both no-agent validation toasts stay direct and the success-less envelope branch stays. `TemplatesPanel` moves save onto a mutation (`saving` retires; the dialog keeps its pending guard) with the new `templates.toast.save_failed`, routes delete through `templates.toast.delete_failed`, and gives the silent load failure a real surface: an inline `ErrorState` + retry (`common.load_failed` fallback) replaces the `setTemplates([])` whose empty list read as "no templates" (the wave-9 `AgentInteractDock` precedent). Counts: `useMutation` call sites 88 → 92, `useErrorToast` importing files 37 → 42, `formatThrownError` action-surface call sites 11 → 1 (the survivor is `ShellTerminal`'s deliberate 409 branch), and 5 new keys land in both locales (4721 → 4726 used keys). Coverage: five new suites (21 tests) — `NtlmPage.test.tsx` (4), `usePivotingData.test.ts` (4), `PivotingPageContent.test.tsx` (2), `TemplatesPanel.test.tsx` (6), `ToolkitPage.test.tsx` (5) — with 11 of the 21 verified red against the pre-migration code (all five suites fail without the change). Local gates green (eslint, `tsc --noEmit`, 808 unit tests across 157 files, the eleven-member `npm run check` chain at 4726 used keys — bundle 3.99MB / 190 files, critical path 970kB, webdist 307 files — and the full static-export Playwright suite 156 passed / 36 skipped); `internal/webdist/dist` was resynced.

- The feedback loop closes on the agents list dock/menu cluster (2026-10 UI/UX batch U3 follow-up, wave 9): the wave-8 note recorded this cluster as never on the U3 map, and it keeps its `e.message ?? generic` fallbacks no longer. `AgentDockCommands` queues ps/screenshot/sleep and starts/stops the SOCKS relay through three mutations while the row `busy` identity stays local in `try/finally`, so the row still disables as one and a rejected relay start/stop surfaces the backend's own message under the `agents.dock_socks_failed` fallback (the hand-rolled promise chains retire). `AgentDockFiles` routes its load/pull/push failures through the normalizer — the inline load error becomes the shared rule instead of the raw message — while the per-row transfer fan-out stays hand-rolled by design: a single mutation's stale-completion guard would drop a sibling transfer's completion, so only the error routing is normalized. `AgentInteractDock` moves approve/reject/cancel onto `toastError` (the generic "Approve failed" copy swallowed every reason) and gives a failed first load a real surface: an inline `ErrorState` + retry replaces the silent `setTasks([])` whose empty list read as "no tasks" (WS-down polling still keeps the last list, so a reconnect cannot flash an error over a working dock). `useAgentMenuAction` normalizes the screenshot and beacon-now rejections (the clipboard failure stays direct — a local capability, not an API error). `useAgentBulkOps` routes its nine failure catches into the action banner through the new shared `errorText`, so a rejected kill surfaces the backend's reason instead of "Kill failed", and the CSV export toast goes through `toastError` while its abort branch keeps the info toast; the page's bulk-results catch follows the same rule. The resolution rule is now single-sourced: `resolveErrorText` in `lib/errors.ts` backs both `useErrorToast` and the new inline `errorText(t, e, () => key)` (the fallback key is a thunk, resolved only when the thrown value carries no mapped code and no backend message), retiring the infrastructure page's local copy. Counts: `useMutation` call sites 85 → 88, `useErrorToast` importing files 32 → 37, the cluster's raw `e.message ?? generic` sites 6 → 0 and its reason-swallowing failure copy 15 → 0. Coverage: four new suites plus two extensions (23 tests — dock command queue and SOCKS rejections, dock files load/pull/push, interact-dock approve/cancel plus the first-load retry, menu-action screenshot/beacon-now, bulk-ops kill/batch/quick-sleep/export, the `errorText` rule); all new-behavior tests verified red against the pre-migration code (17 red / 18 green across the six suites). Local gates green (eslint, `tsc --noEmit`, 787 unit tests, the `npm run check` chain at 4721 used keys — unchanged, no new keys — and the Playwright suite); `internal/webdist/dist` resynced

- The feedback loop closes on the agent sub-pages and the right-rail AI card (2026-10 UI/UX batch U3, wave 8 — the closing wave): the tail named at wave 7 moves onto `useMutation` + `useErrorToast`. `AISuggestCard` (the console's right rail) turns its load and run paths into two mutations: the per-row spinner stays local as an index, a 409 approval notice keeps the header quick-action's `toast.warning(err.message)` treatment, every other rejection routes through the normalizer with the `ai.toast.assist_failed` fallback — the old info-level `e.message` fallback, which rendered genuine failures as neutral info, retires — and an agent switch clears the suggestions and discards an in-flight load. `/agents/:id/config` pushes through a mutation (the dead `res.success` else-branch goes; `api.postJson` already throws for a `success:false` envelope) while its load failure routes through the normalizer and keeps its toast, because the inline `DataError` only renders before the first success — a refresh failure after a push, with the form up, has no other surface — and the route-id effect resets the form and the in-flight push. `/agents/:id/traffic` adapts through mutations: the duplicate load toast retires into the full-page `ErrorState` + retry it doubled, the dead adapt else-branch goes with its now-unused `agents.traffic_adapt_error` key (EN+ZH), the auto-adapt checkbox disables while its toggle is in flight and its target state travels as the mutation argument (a fast double-click previously fired the same flip twice), and the route-id effect clears the previous agent's report. `/agents/:id/token` migrates all seven actions (steal, make, revert, drop, impersonate, whoami, note) with `activeAction` keeping the row/action spinner identity locally; the whoami banner gains an explicit `whoamiError` flag — the old `whoamiResult.startsWith("Error")` styling read unlocalized backend text to decide, so a localized failure rendered as success — and shows the normalized message (falling back to its key), and a switch clears the PID/user/domain/password drafts, the note drafts and the banner. `useScreenMonitor` routes capture/start/stop/title-trigger failures through the normalizer (new `screen.capture_failed` key, EN+ZH), keeps its abort/busy machinery and the trigger-match validation, and leaves the WebSocket `screen_stream_error` text as the event payload it is; `useRemoteDesktop` does the same for start/stop/remote-click while the version-gate validation and the silent background poll stay by design. Two files were audited and left alone with reasons recorded in `docs/UIUX_AUDIT.md`: `ShellPage`'s shell failures already render the inline banner (the silent branch is a quota guard) and `useAgentFiles`'s raw site is a stream `error` event payload, not a promise rejection. Counts: `useMutation` call sites 73 → 85, `useErrorToast` importing files 26 → 32, the tail's hand-rolled API-failure toasts 24 → 0 (the three direct `toast.error` sites left in the six files are two validations and one event text). Coverage: five new suites — `AISuggestCard.test.tsx` (4), `ConfigPage.test.tsx` (4), `TrafficPage.test.tsx` (5), `TokenPage.test.tsx` (4), `useScreenMonitor.test.ts` (4) — plus `useRemoteDesktop.test.ts` 3 → 5, 23 new tests, all verified red against the pre-wave code (21 of the 26 suite tests failed without the change). Gates: eslint, `tsc --noEmit`, 764 unit tests (148 files), the eleven-member `npm run check` chain 11/11 at 4721 used keys (bundle 3.98MB / 190 files, critical path 970kB, webdist 307 files), the full static-export Playwright suite 156 passed / 36 skipped, every visual baseline unmoved; `internal/webdist/dist` was resynced.

- The feedback loop closes on the agent-detail collect and stealth sections (2026-10 UI/UX batch U3, wave 7): the shared dispatcher behind the recon/collect tabs moves onto `useMutation` + `useErrorToast`. `useCollectTask` — the hook that powers nine sections (Recon, Registry, Window, ScreenTrigger, BrowserHistory, WeChat history, Keylogger, Clipboard, Wallpaper) — loses its hand-rolled `try/catch` + `mountedRef`; `collect` and `fire` route failures through the normalizer, and the callers' pre-translated `errorText` becomes an i18n `errorKey` fallback (12 call sites), so a 429/5xx localizes and a 4xx surfaces the backend's own message ("agent is offline") instead of the context copy that overrode every reason — the key remains the last resort for a message-less failure. `fire` grows an options object (`body` / `successText` / `errorKey`) in place of the positional body/success pair, the `busy` identity stays local by design, and a new `onSettled` clears it on both paths. Agent-switch semantics sharpen across the cluster: since `/agents/A → /agents/B` reuses the section instances (the route carries no per-id key), the hook clears the busy identity and the stored dump and retires the in-flight generation on the switch, and a stale completion resolves `null`/`false` for its caller instead of handing the old agent's output to the new section (pinned red); the sections that keep agent-bound local state reset it (clipboard draft + history, registry drafts + confirm, screen-trigger match/watching, window/browser results + filters, keylogger badge, recon label), and the four remaining raw-`e.message` sites in the same tabs move onto the normalizer with their context keys — `EvasionSection` (stealth: the technique travels as a mutation argument so a mid-flight re-selection cannot rename the toast, and the armed confirmation is dropped on a switch), `InjectSection` (the staged shellcode and its confirmation abandon on a switch), `WebcamMicSection` (capture failures surface the backend text; an empty capture keeps the direct local-capability toast) and `ProcessSection` (queue-per-row with the confirm path unchanged) — each retiring its hand-rolled `sending` flag. `ScreenTriggerSection` gains the `agents.trigger_failed` fallback key (EN+ZH) and `check-i18n` recognises `errorKey:` data the way it does `labelKey:`/`i18nKey:`. Counts: `useMutation` call sites 67 → 73, `useErrorToast` importing files 21 → 26, collect/stealth raw API-failure toasts 7 → 0. Coverage: `useCollectTask.test.ts` grows 7 → 14 (mapped-status localization, 4xx backend text, the key fallback on a message-less failure, the agent-switch discard, plus the first `fire` success/failure/busy-drop pins), and four new component suites land — `EvasionSection` (5), `InjectSection` (5), `WebcamMicSection` (5) and `ProcessSection` (5) — every behavioral pin verified red against the pre-wave code. Local gates green (eslint, `tsc --noEmit`, 741 unit tests, the eleven-member `npm run check` chain at 4721 used keys, the full static-export Playwright suite 156 passed / 36 skipped, every visual baseline unmoved); `internal/webdist/dist` was resynced.

- The feedback loop reaches the infrastructure page (2026-10 UI/UX batch U3, wave 6): the whole `/infrastructure` cluster moves onto `useMutation` + `useErrorToast` — the page's five redirector actions (save / delete / test-ssh / generate-then-save / deploy-ssh) and the config hook's three (proxy config generate / ACME provision / profile export) become eight mutations, retiring the hand-rolled `try/catch`/`finally` flags and the four pending booleans (`saving`, `deploying`, `testingSSH`, `generatingModal`) they carried, while the row-scoped delete spinner (`deleting === rd.id`) keeps its identity in local state by design and the clipboard-copy toast stays direct (a local capability failure, not an API error). Rejections surface the backend's own message: the save/delete/test-ssh/generate-modal/provision/export toasts route through the normalizer, and two stale-data paths close — two overlapping config generations can no longer let the first response overwrite the newer output (the mutation guard keeps only the latest; pinned red against the old code), and a failed generation no longer passes silently as a server response (the local fallback template still renders for offline usefulness, but the failure is reported alongside it). The SSH probe's `data.success` else-branch is removed: `api.postJson` already throws an `ApiError` for a `success:false` envelope, so the branch was dead — and its now-unused `infrastructure.toast.ssh_failed` key is deleted; the success toast had been reading a nonexistent `stdout` field of a response that reports reachability in `message`, rendering "SSH OK: undefined" for a reachable endpoint (the copy now interpolates `{message}`). The load failure stops doubling as a toast: the old path toasted "failed to load listeners/redirectors" while the same text already filled the inline `DataError` + retry, so both duplicate toasts are retired. The deploy dialog's failure banner renders a normalized message instead of the raw `e.message`, so a network failure reads as the locale's network error rather than "Failed to fetch". Coverage: new `InfrastructurePageContent.test.tsx` (9 component tests — the inline load error with no duplicate toast, a rejected redirector save surfacing "invalid host" and re-enabling the button, the confirmed delete surfacing the backend message, the rejected and successful SSH probe (reachability message instead of "undefined"), the overlapping-generation race, the failed generation's report + fallback template, ACME failure and the provisioned-path banner, the normalized deploy banner; all nine verified red against the pre-migration code). Counts: `useMutation` call sites 59 → 67, `useErrorToast` importing files 19 → 21, infrastructure raw API-failure toasts 9 → 0. Local gates green (eslint, `tsc --noEmit`, 714 unit tests, the eleven-member `npm run check` chain at 4720 used keys, bundle 3.98MB/190 files, critical path 969kB, webdist 307 files); `internal/webdist/dist` was resynced.

- The feedback loop reaches the phishing page (2026-10 UI/UX batch U3, wave 5): the whole `/phishing` surface moves onto `useMutation` + `useErrorToast` — the page's template save/delete, campaign save/launch/stop/delete and the Entra identity panel's four actions (device-code start/poll, consent start/exchange) become ten mutations, retiring the route's hand-rolled `try/catch` + `finally` flags. Every rejection now surfaces the backend's own message instead of generic copy: the page's six API-failure toasts (template save/delete, campaign save/launch/stop/delete) route through the normalizer — the launch path's old `e instanceof Error ? e.message` fallback printed the generic key for a status-carrying rejection, which is exactly what the API layer throws — and the panel's four `entra_failed` toasts, which swallowed every reason, surface it too. The panel's shared `busy` boolean becomes the OR of four per-action pendings: two actions leaving in one commit window (both buttons enabled before either pending flushes) previously let the first completion re-enable all four controls while the second request was still in flight; the OR keeps them disabled until every request settles (pinned by test, verified red against the old boolean). The page's load failure gets a real surface: the old path toasted once (throttled) and rendered empty tables that read as "nothing configured", so it now shows the inline `DataError` + retry the config/traffic page loads use, and `POLL.toastThrottle` is no longer imported there. Stop failures gain their own copy (`phishing.toast.stop_failed`, EN+ZH) instead of reporting "Failed to save campaign". The launch identity spinner (`launchingId`) stays local by design, and the template-required validation toast stays direct by convention. Coverage: new `PhishingPageContent.test.tsx` (7 component tests — the inline load error + retry, a rejected template save surfacing the backend message with the dialog left open, the successful save close+refresh, the launch double-click guard with a rejected launch's message, a rejected stop, the confirmed delete + refresh, and the direct validation toast) and new `EntraIdentityPanel.test.tsx` (3 tests — a rejected device-code start surfacing the backend message, the two-actions-in-flight OR race, and the polled token JSON render); the load-error, save-rejection, launch-rejection, stop-rejection and both panel cases were verified red against the pre-migration code. Counts: `useMutation` call sites 49 → 59, `useErrorToast` importing files 17 → 19, phishing raw API-failure toasts 10 → 0. Local gates green (eslint, `tsc --noEmit`, 705 unit tests, the eleven-member `npm run check` chain at 4721 used keys, the full static-export Playwright suite 156 passed / 36 skipped, every visual baseline unmoved); `internal/webdist/dist` was resynced.

- The feedback loop reaches the persistence page (2026-10 UI/UX batch U3, wave 4): `/agents/:id/persistence` moves each of its three action paths — list, install, remove — onto `useMutation` + `useErrorToast`, while the per-method spinner (`actionLoading = method` / `remove_${method}`) keeps its identity in local state by design. Rejections now surface the backend's own message: the old `formatThrownError` path printed `[object Object]` for a status-carrying rejection, and the three dead `data.success` else-branches are gone — `api.post` already throws an `ApiError` carrying a `success:false` envelope's `error` text, which `toastError` shows as the message. The list poller trades its shared `listCancelledRef` boolean for a monotonic run id, because the boolean had to be cleared for the new run — which *un-cancelled* the old one, so after an agent switch the previous agent's 10×1.5 s loop kept polling its tasks and could paint that detection output onto the new agent's page (the run is now retired at its next check and the whole path is pinned by test). The refresh control shows a spinner while a run is in flight but stays enabled — a re-click supersedes the run instead of stacking a second poll. The route-id effect drops the whole page identity before the new agent's fetch lands (agent, `loadError`, queued methods, detection output, spinner, and all three mutation guards), because `/agents/A/persistence → /agents/B/persistence` reuses the component instance (the route carries no per-id key) and B briefly rendered A's queue. The duplicate load-failure toast is retired in favour of the inline `DataError` + retry (the config/traffic page loads do not toast either); the page's only remaining direct `toast.error` is the version-blocked validation, which stays direct by convention. Coverage: new `PersistencePage.test.tsx` (6 component tests — the mount list plus its polled output, the confirmed install with its task id, a rejected install surfacing "agent offline" while the local spinner clears, the confirmed remove, the route-id reset, and the superseded-run output guard; the last three were verified red against the pre-migration page). Counts: `useMutation` call sites 46 → 49, `useErrorToast` importing files 16 → 17, persistence-page raw error toasts 7 → 0. Local gates green (eslint, `tsc --noEmit`, 695 unit tests, the eleven-member `npm run check` chain at 4720 used keys, the full static-export Playwright suite 156 passed / 36 skipped, every visual baseline unmoved); `internal/webdist/dist` was resynced (307 files).

- The feedback loop reaches the users page (2026-10 UI/UX batch U3, wave 3): the operator-management surface moves onto `useMutation` + `useErrorToast`. Nine mutations replace the page's hand-rolled try/catch paths — create / update / password submit, force-logout, kick, session revoke, revoke-all, and the row-scoped toggle/delete calls — and the three dialog submits gain the pending guards they never had: a second click lands on a disabled button (the create path could previously fire two requests from one slow double-click) and a failure keeps the dialog open with the typed values. The row-scoped spinner (`actionLoading = uid + "_toggle" / uid + "_delete"`) intentionally stays in local state — the hook tracks pending-ness, not which row was clicked — while the calls underneath still route failures through the normalizer, so a rejected toggle now reports the backend's "cannot disable the last admin" instead of the generic copy. The sessions dialog's `revokeLoading` string is retired: "one revoke at a time" is `revokingSession || revokingAll`, so a completion that lands after a newer revoke cannot re-enable the buttons early, and the promise-chain row handler becomes a plain mutation call. The page's ten raw `toast.error(t(...))` sites (including the secondary roles fetch) are gone. Coverage: new `UsersPage.test.tsx` (3 component tests — the double-submit guard, a 409 create surfacing "username already exists", a failed toggle reporting its message while the row spinner stays local; all three were verified red against the pre-migration page). Counts: `useMutation` call sites 37 → 46, `useErrorToast` importing files 15 → 16, users-page raw error toasts 10 → 0. Local gates green (eslint, `tsc --noEmit`, 689 unit tests, the eleven-member `npm run check` chain at 4720 used keys, the full static-export Playwright suite 156 passed / 36 skipped, every visual baseline unmoved); `internal/webdist/dist` was resynced.

- The feedback loop reaches the agent-detail console (2026-10 UI/UX batch U3, wave 2): the three action hooks behind `/agents/:id` move onto `useMutation` + `useErrorToast` — `useAgentNotes` loses its hand-rolled `saving` flag and `mountedRef` and routes save failures through the normalizer (`toastError(e, "agents.detail_notes_save_failed")`), `useAgentQuickShell` keeps its Enter-key `sendingRef` guard (the hook supersedes stale callbacks but does not drop concurrent calls, so the ref is what stops a double command) while `sending` becomes `isPending`, and `useAgentDangerActions` replaces the identity `busy` string with a boolean OR of five per-action pendings (kill / uninstall / set + clear kill date / migrate) whose failures route through `toastError` with per-action keys while successes resolve from `t()` inside the hook (the caller's ten-string `messages` object is gone; the `await run() → keep-the-dialog-open-on-failure` contract is unchanged). `AgentDetailPage` moves Diagnose and Apply-sleep off their hand-rolled flags too, and the header quick-action handler's generic failure routes through `toastError(..., { label })` (its 409 warning and `logger.error` stay). Route-id semantics sharpen: both per-agent hooks call `reset()` from their id effect, so a completion that lands after the operator switched agents is discarded — it can no longer close the new agent's notes editor, push the old command's result into the new agent's quick-shell history, toast "sleep updated" with the wrong hostname, or leave the old send's guard blocking the new agent (the header's per-button spinner and the persistence page's per-method spinner keep local identity state by design). `check-i18n` now also recognises `toastError(err, "key", params)` fallbacks — the optional `fallbackParams` argument wave 1 introduced; two settings call sites were previously exempt only via the `settings.` dynamic prefix. Coverage: new `useAgentNotes.test.ts` (success closes the editor + reloads, mapped failure keeps it open, a mid-flight id change abandons the save) and `useAgentQuickShell.test.ts` (output recorded then input cleared, a second send is dropped while one is in flight, a mid-flight id change discards the result and unblocks the new agent), plus the extended `useAgentDangerActions.test.ts` (boolean outcome contract, 403 → mapped `common.forbidden`, busy across the in-flight window, no-agent guard) — 12 hook tests, and the two abandon cases were verified to fail against the pre-reset form. Counts: `useMutation` call sites 28 → 37, `useErrorToast` importing files 12 → 15, hand-rolled error toasts in the console's action paths 9 → 0. Local gates green (eslint, `tsc --noEmit`, 686 unit tests, the eleven-member `npm run check` chain at 4720 used keys, the full static-export Playwright suite 156 passed / 36 skipped, every visual baseline unmoved).

- Route changes in the console cross-fade instead of hard-cutting (2026-10 UI/UX batch U5, M1): `frontend/src/router.tsx` wraps `router.navigate` — the single funnel every navigation passes through, so the `<Link>` sites and the programmatic `navigate()` calls stay untouched — to default the navigation's `viewTransition` option into the View Transitions API whose `::view-transition` rules (and reduced-motion override) `globals.css` already carried as dead code. The default is spelled `options?.viewTransition ?? true` because `useLinkClickHandler` forwards a present-but-undefined `viewTransition` key on every link click — an unconditional spread clobbers it and link navigation fires no transition at all (measured before the fix: the navigation completed and `startViewTransition` stayed at 0 calls); an explicit `false` opt-out is respected untouched, and the wrapper skips the API entirely when `document.startViewTransition` is missing (jsdom, older browsers) or when the user prefers reduced motion (`prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`), checked before opting in so React Router's own warn-once fallback never runs. Coverage: `src/router-transitions.test.ts` pins the four wrapper decisions against a spied `createBrowserRouter` (undefined key → `true`, explicit `false` kept, reduced motion skipped, API absent skipped) and fails against the unconditional-spread form, while the Playwright `route transitions` spec drives the real `/chat → /ai` client-side redirect in Chromium on all four viewports, asserting the API is called (> 0) and skipped under emulated reduced motion (0). Local gates green (eslint, `tsc --noEmit`, 679 unit tests, the eleven-member `npm run check` chain, the full static-export Playwright suite 156 passed / 36 skipped, every visual baseline unmoved).

- The feedback loop lands its first wave (2026-10 UI/UX batch U3): the settings tree moved off hand-rolled mutation state — all 22 handlers across `pages/settings/**` now use the `useMutation` hook (8 in `SettingsPage` — agent/server/malleable saves, password change, JWT regeneration, vacuum, backup download, purge — plus `useTOTP` ×3, `CertificatesSection` ×2, `EmergencySection`, `BackupSection` ×2, `ModulesSection` ×3, `NotificationsSection`, `SIEMRulesSection` and `ApiKeysSection`), replacing ten boolean `setSaving`-style flags with per-action `isPending` so a slow first response can no longer clobber a newer one and unmounts are guarded; the sections' shared `saving` prop is now derived from the mutations (disabled-state behaviour unchanged), and the hand-rolled 10-second `withSaveTimeout` wrapper is retired along with its `settings.toast.save_timeout` key — the API layer's own 30 s timeout plus `toastError` covers the stuck-button case. Error feedback converges too: `useErrorToast` gains an optional `fallbackParams` argument (the `{ action }` emergency template interpolates through it), the last four raw `err.message` toasts in the tree are gone, three settings saves that previously failed **silently** (the old wrapper let the rejection escape as an unhandled rejection with no toast) now report, SIEM toggle/delete no longer swallow failures, and the SIEM save error shows its own `settings.toast.siem_save_failed` key instead of the ExtC2 `channel_config_failed` copy-paste (4 new EN+ZH keys total); per-row identity spinners (`restoring`/`downloading`/`savingId`/`testingId`) intentionally keep local state, because the hook tracks pending-ness, not which row was clicked. Counts: `useMutation` call sites 6 → 28, `useErrorToast` importing files 2 → 12, settings-tree raw-message error toasts 4 → 0. Local gates green (eslint, `tsc --noEmit`, 675 unit tests — the new `fallbackParams` case included — the eleven-member `npm run check` chain at 4718 used keys, the full static e2e including all 48 dual-theme visual baselines: 136 passed / 40 skipped by design, zero failures), `internal/webdist/dist` was resynced, and `docs/UIUX_AUDIT.md` records the wave-1 state and the batch-map row

- The keyboard and accessible-name tail is closed (2026-10 UI/UX batch U2): the screen-viewer overlay's two unnamed buttons are gone — play/pause carries a state-dependent `aria-label` and fullscreen a static one (new `agents.screen_play` / `agents.screen_pause` / `agents.screen_fullscreen` keys in EN+ZH) — the campaign phase chips expose the full phase name via `aria-label` instead of relying on the truncated visible label plus a `title` tooltip, the breadcrumb's icon-only dashboard link is named from its item label, and the duplicated skip link is down to one: the static `index.html` copy is deleted so the localized React link (same `#main-content` target, `<main tabIndex={-1}>`) is the single pre-content tab stop. Scrollable tables can now be named: `Table` takes an optional `label` and, when present, renders its scroll container as `role="region"` + `aria-label` + explicit `tabIndex={0}` (Chromium focuses overflowing scrollers implicitly; the explicit index keeps horizontal scroll keyboard-reachable in every engine), with the listeners table the first opt-in. `check:aria` graduates to a strict member of `npm run check` and now fails on unnamed **and** title-only icon triggers (184 scanned, both zero); a temporary tab-walk probe against the static export confirmed the new order — one skip link, named video controls, named breadcrumb link, named table region. Local gates green (eslint, `tsc --noEmit`, 674 unit tests, the eleven-member `npm run check` chain)
- The U1 contrast work reaches its final state on the page layer (2026-10 UI/UX batch U1b): every status-colored call site now uses the role tokens the U1a pass introduced — 300 bare `text-X` classes in 104 page/component files became `text-X-ink` (scripted regex migration with a negative lookahead so `-foreground`/`-ink` and alpha forms are never re-wrapped, and the single deliberate exception of the `ScreenVideoPlayer` overlay dots, which paint on a dark video surface), 38 `bg-X/NN text-X-foreground` chips/banners moved off the solid-fill ink onto the tint-safe ink (28 detected mechanically as same-status tint+foreground lines, ten context-read by hand: `AgentDockShot`, the `AgentHeader` AV chip, `TokenPage` process-error, the generate banner, the listener callback strip, `LateralPageContent`, the `ListenersPageContent` burned-count note, the `PhishingPageContent` stop button, `SecuritySection`'s JWT box, `EventsStream` stream errors), and the four light `bg-primary/10 hover:bg-primary/20` text buttons drop the hover to `/15` (a `/20` fill lands at 4.42:1 over the sidebar; `/15` is 4.81). `check:contrast` graduates from report-mode prototype to a strict member of `npm run check` (`--strict` baked into the script; the pair matrix drops the now-unused bare-`text-X` and foreground-on-tint pairs and takes the primary tint percentages per theme — light 10/12/15, dark 10/12/15/20). Final readings: 304 token pairs in both themes, **0 text failures** (the audit-time baseline was 114), with the 2 non-text observations (ring/chart marks) and 6 informational boundaries unchanged; local gates green (eslint, `tsc --noEmit`, 674 unit tests, the ten-member `npm run check` chain, 50/50 dual-theme win32 visual baselines — text-color changes stay inside the 1.5 % pixel tolerance, so no baseline regeneration). `docs/UIUX_AUDIT.md` records the final D1 state and the batch-map row
- The status color system is rebuilt around explicit ink/solid roles (2026-10 UI/UX batch U1a): `--X` keeps the base hue (fills, borders, dots), the new `--X-solid` carries the full-strength chip/button fill (dark theme darkens `warning` 0.78→0.45 and `destructive` 0.64→0.55 so light ink keeps contrast — the dark solid-warning pair measured 1.75:1 and is now 6.47:1), `--X-foreground` is the ink placed **on** a solid fill (light `success`/`info` swap their near-white value for the same dark ink the dark theme already used, which repairs the 1.2:1 light badges at the token level, and `--destructive-foreground` exists now, so the six `text-destructive-foreground` call sites stop silently inheriting `foreground` — 3.69:1 light / 3.01:1 dark becomes 4.90:1 / 5.19:1), and the new `--X-ink` is the text-safe status color for neutral surfaces and `bg-X/8..20` tints. Every primitive moved to the right role — badge/banner/alert/error-state/button/dropdown-menu variants, the sidebar count and lab chips, the agent status banner, session-timeout warning, update banner/dialog, notification dropdown, TopBar reconnect strip, task renderers and the `statusStyles` tone/hue maps — and the six phantom-class sites (`TrafficPage` clear-log, `AgentBulkBar` ×3, `InfrastructurePageContent` haproxy, `ProfilesPageContent` delete) plus the dark solid-warning pair (campaign phase chip, Infrastructure apache button) use the solid tokens. Light `--primary` nudges 0.53 → 0.505 (active-nav 4.4 → 5.0) and `avatar-4`/`avatar-7` darken a step so initials clear 4.5:1; dark-theme values are unchanged apart from the new solid fills. Browser-cross-checked in Chromium (temporary canvas/pixel harness): every ink pair ≥ 5.0 light / 6.0 dark, solid fills 4.62–8.37, no regressions; `check:contrast` now composites in gamma-encoded sRGB (the space browsers actually blend in — it agrees with the pixel harness within ~0.1) and models the ink/solid pairs, with the remaining reds being the page-level legacy pattern owned by U1b. `docs/frontend-style-conventions.md` documents the four roles; visual baselines do not move (50/50 green without snapshot updates)
- The UI/UX program opens with an evidence batch (2026-10 audit): `docs/UIUX_AUDIT.md` records the seven-dimension baseline (contrast, keyboard/names, feedback, narrow screens, motion, copy, visual consistency) with ~20 defect clusters owned by the U1–U6 fix batches, and two report-mode scanners land beside the existing gates — `frontend/scripts/check-contrast.mjs` (WCAG ratios over 288 token pairs in both themes, `--selftest` pins the color math, `--strict` ready for U1) and `frontend/scripts/check-aria.mjs` (icon-trigger accessible-name scan across 184 triggers, `--strict` ready for U2), run via `npm run check:contrast` / `check:aria`. Headline findings, all browser-cross-checked: `text-destructive-foreground` is a phantom utility (6 call sites silently inherit `foreground` → 3.69:1 light / 3.01:1 dark on solid destructive), the light `success`/`info` badges render near-white ink on pale tints (1.2:1, effectively invisible), and dark solid `warning` buttons land at 1.75:1. Zero pixel changes — visual baselines do not move.
- The shared automation layer gets its next test cohort (2026-10 hygiene): 41 tests across `completions` (os-scoped catalogues, case-insensitive prefix matching, subcommand filtering incl. multi-word prefixes, the empty-input first-20), `shell` (beacon-timing defaults/coercion/error fallback, localStorage history dedupe + 100-entry cap + blocked-storage survival), `useLiveTaskResult` (the live `pending_approval` → `sent` → `completed` frames with mid-flight `result`, `taskId`/`running` exposure, cancelled → failed with the `common.task_failed` fallback, "did not respond" → timeout, unmount abort, reset vs stale-completion seq guard) and `agent-files/file-transfer` (single/chunked/unknown-size pull plans with progress offsets, partial-at-cap, missing task ACK and server-error passthrough, pre-/mid-pull abort, plus the push path through a fake `XMLHttpRequest`: 50 MiB pre-flight cap, CSRF header/credentials/timeout, upload-progress percentages only when computable, 401 → the central unauthorized flow, non-2xx/network/timeout errors, abort signal, and ACK task polling). The statement-coverage floor ratchets 16.0% → 16.5% (measured 17.64%, 10050/56968).
- The `check:bundle` critical-path budget tightens 1,200 KiB → 1,090 KiB (2026-10 maintenance): batches S/T brought the first-paint path to 968 KiB (7 chunks) and held it there, so the old ~230 KiB slack would have let one mid-size page stop being lazy and still pass; ~122 KiB remains for ordinary entry/vendor growth while the 100s-of-kB route re-leak class still trips. The 5.50 MB total budget is deliberately unchanged — unlike the critical path it grows with every added page (measured 3.97 MB / 190 files), and the asymmetry is now noted in `docs/FRONTEND_PERF.md`.
- The shared hook/utility layer gets its first tests (2026-10 hygiene): 42 tests across `useForm` (raw-string number inputs, blur errors, the `submittingRef` double-submit guard, rejected-submit toast), `usePersistedState` (corrupt JSON fallback, key-change resync, quota-safe writes), `useVirtualWindow` (threshold, measure, single-rAF throttle, unmount cleanup), `useErrorToast` (aborted errors stay silent, mapped keys through `t`, raw/fallback messages), the theme provider (stored-theme boot, dark-class toggles, system + `matchMedia` changes), `highlight` (json/xml/table/error/plain branches) and `chart-palette`. The statement-coverage floor ratchets 15.5% → 16.0% (measured 17.14%, 9766/56968). `frontend/scripts/add-toolkit-keys.mjs` is deleted — a completed one-off whose anchor lines still exist in `zh.ts`, so a rerun would duplicate keys; nothing referenced it (`unify-icon-sizes.mjs` stays: the style guide cites it as the mechanical-migration reference).
- The i18n provider gets its first unit tests (2026-10 hygiene): `frontend/src/lib/i18n/index.test.tsx` pins the en default and key fallback for missing entries, the zh boot-from-localStorage path (dynamic dictionary load, `documentElement.lang` swap, `getLocale()` mirror), and the setLocale flow — G2's rotated `t` identity, the localStorage + cookie persistence, and the same-locale no-op. The zh dictionary is stubbed so the ~200 KB module never loads; the missing-key probe passes the key via a variable because `check-i18n.mjs` scans test files too.
- Local win32 visual baselines are refreshed (2026-10 maintenance): thirteen `frontend/e2e/layout.spec.ts` snapshots (settings/ai/chat/agents across laptop+mobile) had drifted by a few percent of pixels from font/rasterizer changes on the current host and now match the shipped UI — the layout set is green locally for the first time since 2026-08-29 (68 passed / 32 skipped). The Linux baseline set CI enforces was already current and is untouched; `maxDiffPixelRatio` stays at 0.015.
- The frontend gates ratchet with batch U (2026-10 batch U): the statement-coverage floor moves 14.8% → 15.5% (measured 16.46%, 9376/56968 — the R–T batches' test additions; the ~1-point headroom is deliberate, a large new component shipping without tests still trips it), and the static-export Playwright smoke grows a real `/generate` probe: it forces `forgec2_lang=en`, asserts the route's five tabs plus the payload panel's own lazy chunk (the *Agent Binaries* format picker cannot exist before `GeneratePayloadWorkspace` loads), then switches to Builds and checks the `?tab=builds` deep link and `aria-selected`. Three candidates were evaluated and deliberately not done — recorded in `docs/FRONTEND_PERF.md`: `/api` JSON compression (re-checked, still no measured win, and the beacon hot path stays allocation-free), a `?v=` favicon version stamp (batch R's `no-cache` + boot-time content-hash ETag already revalidates to 304/0 B; a query stamp would just fork the URL), and npm-izing the vendored vis-network pair (the on-demand `/js` fetch with the batch S watchdog is what keeps a 466 KiB renderer + 215 KiB stylesheet out of the Vite graph and off the route chunks).
- The operator console sheds its dead code and pins its formatting, selector and async-mutation conventions (2026-10 batch T): the standalone chat page — unreachable since the AI workspace absorbed chat — is deleted along with its 14 `chat.*` i18n keys (the `/chat` → `/ai` redirect and the static-export shell stay), and `npm run audit:unused-exports` now reports **zero** files (28 before): six dead definitions are gone (`isLiveProcessTree`, `resolvedGenerateFormat`, `TaskTerminalStatus`, `clearNewAISessionDraft`, `emptyMalleableForm`, `EVENT_COLORS`), `lib/shell.ts` now imports `SHELL_HISTORY_KEY` from `lib/shell-ui.ts` instead of carrying a second copy of the storage-key literal, and the remaining 29 symbols that never left their module drop the `export` keyword. Dates and numbers format through the new `src/lib/intl.ts` helpers (`formatDateTime` / `formatDateOnly` / `formatTimeOnly` / `formatNumber`) against the **UI language** (`getLocale()` from the i18n provider, mirrored wherever a locale is committed) instead of the browser default — ten bare `toLocale*` call sites migrated (notification timestamps, media/clipboard stamps, traffic sizes, AI session/message times, token and download counts, reload stamps), invalid input degrades to `-`, and `lib/utils.ts` (`formatTime`, `nowTime`) funnels into the same helpers; the sixteen partial `@/lib/i18n` test mocks gained `getLocale` so the new dependency cannot break them silently. Accessibility: `TableHead` renders `scope="col"` by default; a repo-wide label spot-check fixed three real gaps (the credentials type input, the SIEM enabled switch, the plugin execute-target select) and recorded the still-dominant legacy sibling-label pattern in `docs/FRONTEND_CONVENTIONS.md`. Store selectors that return stored references lost three redundant `useShallow` wrappers (`stats`, `onlineUsers` — `Object.is` is already the right equality); `ExtC2Section` is the reference migration to `useMutation` + `useErrorToast` (new `settings.toast.channel_remove_failed` key, delete failures are no longer swallowed), and RolesPage is the skeleton pilot (`PageContainer loadingSkeleton` with layout-matched card rows). Critical path 969→968 kB, total still 3.98 MB (190 files) under the unchanged `check:bundle` ceilings.
- The operator shell loads ~112 KB less before first paint (2026-10 batch S): the login page is a lazy route now, so zod — pulled in through the form schema — leaves the critical path with it, and the base-ui vendor chunk is split by *measured* reachability instead of module size (a probe build isolated every `@base-ui/*` subpath, and the entry's static import closure decided which subpaths no entry-reachable module imports; those 14 keys ship as the lazy `vendor-baseui-forms`, everything else stays preloaded). A 401 starts the login chunk before the redirect (`frontend/src/lib/login-preload.ts`), so an expired session still lands on a page that is already fetching. Navigation prefetches on sidebar/command-palette hover or focus through an import map shared with the router (`route-prefetch.ts` + `route-imports.ts`), three likely-next routes warm on idle (skipped entirely on save-data connections), and the topology graph now reports a failed vis-network fetch (`onerror` + a 15 s watchdog) with a working retry instead of leaving a blank canvas — the retry clears the fallback first, because while it is shown the graph container is unmounted and a naive retry could never re-mount it. Measured: critical path 1,104,896 → 992,406 raw (1,079 → 969 KiB against the `check:bundle` 1,200 KiB ceiling), shell + CSS gzip 368,034 → 337,266, and `out/index.html` never preloads `vendor-baseui-forms`; the loading contract, numbers and verification commands are in `docs/FRONTEND_PERF.md`
- The embedded frontend is served under a real cache and compression contract (2026-10 batch R): Vite's content-addressed `/assets/*` files go out `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` (the dead `/_next/static/` immutable branch is gone — this build has never emitted that prefix), while every name-stable file (`index.html`, the per-route HTML shells, the vendored `/js` and `/css` bundles, `favicon.ico`) is served `no-cache` with a content-hash ETag computed once at boot, because `embed.FS` carries no mtime to offer as a Last-Modified. A matching `If-None-Match` — exact, weak (`W/`) or `*` — answers 304/0 B, and dropping `no-store` from the shell restores bfcache eligibility. Text bodies ≥1 KiB are gzipped on the fly through a pooled writer when the client negotiates it (`Vary: Accept-Encoding` on every negotiable response, explicit `gzip;q=0` refusals honoured), with compression skipped for Range and HEAD requests, binary formats and sub-threshold files; the whole path lives inside the SPA static middleware, so `/api`, `/ws`, `/extc2`, the beacon/health routes and the malleable-profile safety net are untouched. Measured on the real server: index chunk 394,657 → 110,807 B, `vis-network.min.js` 476,620 → 128,752 B, the `/` shell 2,257 → 880 B, and a shell revisit revalidates to a 304 in 0 B. `docs/FRONTEND_PERF.md` records the contract, the measurements and the verification commands
- Backups became observable and tunable: `backup.retain` (was a hard-coded 7 snapshots) and `backup.schedule` are configurable via config or `FORGEC2_BACKUP_RETAIN` / `FORGEC2_BACKUP_SCHEDULE`; `GET /api/v1/health` reports `backup.{retain,age_seconds,last_success,last_failure,last_error}` and Prometheus gains `forgec2_backup_age_seconds`, `forgec2_backup_success_total`, `forgec2_backup_failures_total`, so a silently stalled backup job is visible instead of discovered at restore time
- Built-in database backup/restore/VACUUM are explicitly SQLite-only: on other drivers the endpoints answer `501` with guidance (use `pg_dump`/`pgBackRest`) and the scheduled backup job stays disabled instead of failing cryptically
- CI: `internal/scripting` + `cmd` packages are now part of the test run, a 33% statement-coverage floor is enforced (current total ~35%), a dedicated `-race` job covers server/plugin/scripting/agent/db, a weekly fuzz campaign exercises the beacon and malleable fuzz targets, and a new job validates all compose files plus the production overlay and builds the image
- Release: CycloneDX SBOM (`forgec2-server-linux-amd64.sbom.json`) and SLSA-style build provenance attestations ship with tag releases, and release notes are lifted from the matching `CHANGELOG.md` section
- The fleet kill-switch endpoint now dispatches to enrolled agents in one batched insert instead of per-agent round trips (measured ≈2× faster and ≈4× fewer allocations at 1,000 agents); pending caps, tenant inheritance, escalation flags and the uninstall counter behave exactly as before
- CI: the golangci-lint gate runs as an incremental ratchet (`fetch-depth: 2` + `--new-from-rev=HEAD~`) so new code is held to the standard while existing debt shrinks, the frontend gate enforces visual baselines on Linux and a measured coverage floor, and the smoke suite no longer asserts retired static routes
- Listener detail reads no longer sort the whole fleet: `implants.listener_id` had no composite with `last_seen`, so `WHERE listener_id = ? ORDER BY last_seen DESC LIMIT 5000` (and the active counters that reuse the predicate with a `last_seen` window) fell back to a temp b-tree sort and per-row lookups. The new `idx_implants_listener_seen` migration turns both into index walks, and the SQL-plan harness now pins one migration per probe — an index added to an already-applied migration would never reach existing databases
- Benchmark rejected after measurement: GORM's `PrepareStmt` statement cache was tried against the three beacon benchmarks because SQL parsing looked like free money in the CPU profile; it came out 1–9% slower with more allocations on every shape and was not shipped. The A/B numbers are in `docs/CAPACITY.md` so the next batch does not repeat the experiment
- The remote-desktop page treats the dashboard WebSocket as the primary frame transport: while pushed frames keep arriving the HTTP fallback poll is skipped (grace of max(5s, 2.5 poll periods)), and the shortcut resets when monitoring stops so a new session still gets its forced first capture — the same pattern the Screen tab already used
- The Go agent's full-image sleep masks are retired, and the alert chain built around them goes with them: `advanced`/`foliage`/`zilean` encrypted every committed page synchronously from the running goroutine — code pages, stack and Go heap included — which crashes the process the instant masking engages, and the Go runtime keeps executing from other threads during sleep, so no pure-Go variant of that technique is sound. The agent-side integrity reporter and the server-side `autoSwitchSleepMask` chain go too — the latter parsed a `mask=<name>` format the agent never emitted and rotated through the retired masks (`RecordIntegrityFailure` stays: the beacon env-threat and honeypot escalation still count through it). `set_sleep_mask_advanced` keeps its task type and endpoint and now answers with the reason instead of claiming a switch; full-image masking belongs to the C implant, whose Ekko-style mask is untouched
- uTLS JA3 rotation is folded into the default agent build: the `chameleon` build tag that no build path or CI job ever set is retired, the rotator is wired at startup, and rotation stays off by default so the shipped fingerprint remains the stable Chrome Auto a real browser presents (`ChameleonStr=true` at build time or `FORGEC2_CHAMELEON=1` at runtime turns it on; profile via `ChameleonProfileStr` / `FORGEC2_CHAMELEON_PROFILE`, default `random` per dial). The documented short profile names (`chrome`, `firefox`, `safari`, `edge`, `ios`, `android`, `360`, `qq`) now actually resolve — they never matched the full utls names before — unknown names fall back to Chrome Auto, and the dead 12–36 h rotation-interval bookkeeping is gone
- The npm dependency line is current and future groups stay reviewable (2026-10 dependency batch, closing #23): Dependabot's `npm-deps` group now carries `update-types: ["minor", "patch"]`, so semver-major bumps arrive as individual PRs instead of reddening a 32-item batch — #23 mixed TypeScript 7 into the group and failed `npm ci` on `openapi-typescript@7.13.0`'s `typescript@^5.x` peer. The compatible subset landed: React/react-dom 19.3.0 with matching `@types`, react-router-dom 7.18.4, lucide-react 1.57, dompurify 3.4.17 (with `@types/dompurify` 3.2), zod 4.6.7, zustand 5.0.15, sonner 2.0.8, tailwind-merge 3.7.0, `@playwright/test` 1.64.1 (browsers re-installed for the new revision), the testing-library and Tailwind/PostCSS patches, shadcn 4.21.5, globals 17.13 and typescript-eslint 8.71. Base UI stays on the 1.6 line (`~1.6.0`): 1.9.0 makes `vendor-baseui` and `vendor-baseui-forms` import each other, which throws `Cannot access 'we' before initialization` before the SPA mounts, so the pin and the reason sit next to `BASEUI_PAGE_ONLY_KEYS`. TypeScript 5.x, Vite 7, Vitest 3, ESLint 9, jsdom 25 and `@types/node` 20 stay put; those majors are the next batches. Gates: `tsc --noEmit` and eslint clean, 883 unit tests, coverage 35.42% against the 16.5% floor, `npm run check` 11/11 (bundle 4.05 MB / 190 files, critical path 1013 kB of 1090 kB), dist re-synced (337 files), full e2e 184 passed / 36 skipped against the committed baselines, `check:audit` clean (production high/critical zero; the usual 8 dev-only highs recorded), and `npm ci --dry-run` accepts the lock
- The dependency line closed its remaining safe items (2026-10 follow-up): the Go group moved to quic-go 0.64.0 and modernc.org/sqlite (merged from #28; `go build ./...` and the full package test set pass in a clean worktree, with the local WIP tenant test left untouched), the Docker base moved to node 26.12-alpine (#27), react/react-dom moved to the 19.3.1 patch with the dist rebuilt (types stay at 19.3.0), and Dependabot now ignores `@base-ui/react` `>=1.7.0 <2.0.0` — 1.9.0's internal graph makes `vendor-baseui` and `vendor-baseui-forms` import each other, so the `~1.6.0` pin and the ignore both stay until `BASEUI_PAGE_ONLY_KEYS` is re-measured. The npm patch re-ran tsc, eslint, 883 unit tests, the coverage floor, `npm run check` 11/11 (bundle 4.05 MB, critical path 1013 kB), the 337-file dist sync and e2e 184/36; #29 is closed as superseded
- ESLint moved to 10.13.0 and `@eslint/js` to 10.0.1; the latter is now a direct devDependency because ESLint 10 no longer ships it transitively and `eslint.config.mjs` already imported it. The new recommended `no-useless-assignment` rule flagged seven dead initializers/assignments in `ClientProvider`, `AIPageContent`, `dock-commands` and `import-target`; all were removed as behavior-neutral cleanups rather than disabling the rule. The npm lock changed and the dist was rebuilt (337 files), with tsc, lint, 883 tests, coverage 35.42%, `npm run check` 11/11 (bundle 4.05 MB, critical path 1013 kB), e2e 184/36 and audit all green
- The frontend toolchain floor moved to Node 24.15+ (CI, release and visual-baseline workflows now use `24`; Docker already builds on 26.12), unlocking Vitest 5.0.5, `@vitest/coverage-v8` 5.0.5, jsdom 30.1.3 and concurrently 10.0.6, with `@types/node` aligned to `^24.19.2`. All 883 tests pass under the new runner; the v8 provider now counts ~22.6k statements instead of ~57.5k, so the reported coverage is 34.03% (still far above the unchanged 16.5% floor) — the metric change is instrumentation, not lost tests. The build output is byte-identical (`index-QrRZhlkh.js`, 337-file dist), so no dist or server redeploy was needed; tsc, lint, `npm run check` 11/11 (bundle 4.05 MB, critical path 1013 kB), e2e 184/36, audit and `npm ci --dry-run` all pass
- Vite moved to 8.3.4 with `@vitejs/plugin-react` 6.1.2 (Rolldown/Oxc). The config is ESM now (`"type": "module"`, `vitest.config.ts` uses `import.meta.url`, and `staticExport` is imported with an explicit `.js` extension so Vite's native config loader is warning-free). The static-export preload filter follows the entry's static import closure instead of a `vendor-*` prefix, so the new 236 kB shared `utils` chunk (English dictionary) is preloaded while route chunks stay lazy. Output: total 4.11 MB / 197 JS+CSS files (vs 4.05/190), critical path 1007 kB / 13 chunks (vs 1013/7), dist 344 files (vs 337). Gates: tsc, lint, 883 tests, coverage 34.01%, `npm run check` 11/11, e2e 184/36, audit and `npm ci --dry-run`; the initial install needed `--legacy-peer-deps` for plugin-react 6's optional Babel peer against the project's Babel 7 (the Babel path is unused, Oxc is the default), and the committed lock installs cleanly with plain `npm install`/`npm ci`
- Dependabot ignores `@types/node >=25.0.0 <27.0.0` while the frontend floor stays Node 24.15 (CI/release use `24`); Node 25/26 types would advertise APIs the supported runtime does not provide
- web-vitals moved to 6.2.3. The telemetry collector only uses the `Metric` type plus `onCLS/onFCP/onINP/onLCP/onTTFB`, all of which keep their signatures in v6; the lazy web-vitals chunk and the entry were rebuilt. Output: total 4.12 MB / 197 files, critical path 1010 kB / 13 chunks (still far under the 5.50 MB / 1090 kB budgets), dist 344 files. Gates: tsc, lint, 883 tests, coverage 34.01%, `npm run check` 11/11, e2e 184/36, audit and `npm ci --dry-run` all pass; #36 is closed as superseded
- @base-ui/react moved from 1.6.0 to 1.9.0 under Vite 8. Rolldown no longer makes vendor-baseui and vendor-baseui-forms import each other (verified 0/0), and the 1.9.0 e2e run passes with no TDZ, so the `~1.6.0` pin and its Dependabot ignore are gone; BASEUI_PAGE_ONLY_KEYS still keeps page-only form primitives out of first paint. Output: total 4.12 MB / 197 JS+CSS files, critical path 1015 kB / 13 chunks (under 5.50 MB / 1090 kB), dist 344 files. Gates: tsc, lint, 883 tests, coverage 34.01%, `npm run check` 11/11, e2e 184/36, audit and `npm ci --dry-run` all pass
- Linux Playwright baselines were regenerated on `ubuntu-latest` from the Vite 8/base-ui 1.9 build (`visual-baselines.yml`, run 39364467189, `--update-snapshots=all` as prescribed). 27 of 28 PNGs changed; the win32 baselines were not touched, and dashboard/login samples were visually checked for a valid render
- The Vite config moved from the deprecated `build.rollupOptions` alias to `build.rolldownOptions` (Vite 8.3 / Rolldown 1.2). The build output is byte-identical (344 files, critical 1015 kB), so there is no dist or deployment change
- `frontend/package.json` declares `"engines": { "node": ">=24.15.0" }`, matching the CI/release/visual Node 24 floor
- Dependabot holds TypeScript at 5.x (ignore `>=6.0.0 <8.0.0`): `openapi-typescript@7.13.0` peers `^5.x`, so 6.0.3/7.0.2 cannot be adopted yet; `typescript-eslint@8.72.0` would accept 6.0.x (`<6.1.0`) but not 7
- U7 residue: the Plugins execute dialog's fallback literals now say "Target Beacon" / "Select target beacon" instead of "Target Agent" / "Select an agent (optional)". The live strings were already correct (the i18n keys exist); this only removes a latent banned term for missing translations
- Archived the four U6 evidence captures (Payload Generator, Toolkit, Network Topology, PrivEsc) under `docs/design/u6/` and linked them from the audit as pre-U7 before evidence; docs-only, no code or dist change
- Removed the deprecated `@types/dompurify` stub (3.2.0): `dompurify@3.4.17` ships its own declarations, so the stub only added a redundant `dompurify: *` dependency and an outdated-package warning; no runtime, bundle or dist change. The lockfile also picked up the `engines.node >=24.15.0` metadata from the earlier `package.json` change

## [2.6.2] - 2026-09-24

### Security

- **Six 5xx responses echoed raw internal errors, and a gate now prevents it.** The generation handlers answered 500 with `err.Error()` for v3 registration-secret and implant-config failures, and the reverse-port-forward bind error included the raw socket message — all of which can name key material, absolute paths or local addresses. They now log the real error and answer with `sanitizeError`. `scripts/check-error-sanitization.mjs` (`check:error-sanitization` in `npm run check`) walks every `respondError(..., StatusInternalServerError, …)` call and fails when the message is a raw error rather than a literal or a sanitizer; verified in both directions with a probe file. It scanned 1,277 5xx call sites clean after the change.

- Server security debt (P2): force-logout/disable revoke live operator WebSocket sessions; rate-limit login and public downloads; redact URL secrets in request logs; gate screenshots; expand audit `shouldLogAction` coverage; RD route permission gates + tenant checks
- OTA closed loop (P4-4): generate-time ldflags pin `updatePinnedPubKeyHex`; wire `SetUpdateSigningKeyFile` to `server.data_dir`; `crypto.require_release_signature` fail-closed hot-update gate (hot-reloadable); self-update verify failures classified as Error without success exit
- QUIC/gRPC listener hardening (P4-1): stream caps, keepalive/`MaxConnectionAge`, accept-loop backoff, send/recv size limits
- Production compose hardening (C1): `docker-compose.prod.yml` overlay with `read_only` rootfs, tmpfs for `/tmp`+`/home/nonroot`, memory/pids limits, required `FORGEC2_JWT_SECRET`, storage key passthrough, default bind `0.0.0.0`
- Optional session AEAD (C2): `crypto.session_cipher` selects AES-GCM (default) or ChaCha20-Poly1305; negotiated via handshake `sc` field; unknown config values normalize to AES-GCM; agents without `sc` stay on AES-GCM

### Added

- Maturity blueprint `docs/MATURITY_BLUEPRINT.md` (Phase A) with Phase B P4-* mapping
- Generated command reference `docs/COMMAND_REFERENCE.md` via `scripts/gen-command-reference.mjs` (+ `--check` freshness gate)
- Generated capability-matrix task inventory via `scripts/gen-capability-matrix.mjs` (+ CI `--check` with command-reference)
- Plugin Unix process-group kill (Setpgid) alongside Windows Job Object (P4-3)
- CI: `./internal/payload/...` tests; darwin amd64/arm64 agent cross-compile smoke; tag releases require `RELEASE_SIGNING_KEY`
- `scripts/build-agent.ps1` multi-GOOS matrix (linux/darwin)
- Root `VERSION` file as single source for docs/matrix version stamp

### Changed

- gofmt debt cleared across `internal`, `cmd`, `pkg` (P3)
- Engine hardening: Makefile vet gate, security docs, setup-dev keys, go mod tidy, drop orphan scripts
- Capability matrix refreshed for v2.6.1 transports/plugins/OTA; v2.6.2 header stamped from `VERSION`
- Docs, README, and OpenAPI version bumped to 2.6.2

## [2.4.1] - 2026-07-25

### Security

- **Six 5xx responses echoed raw internal errors, and a gate now prevents it.** The generation handlers answered 500 with `err.Error()` for v3 registration-secret and implant-config failures, and the reverse-port-forward bind error included the raw socket message — all of which can name key material, absolute paths or local addresses. They now log the real error and answer with `sanitizeError`. `scripts/check-error-sanitization.mjs` (`check:error-sanitization` in `npm run check`) walks every `respondError(..., StatusInternalServerError, …)` call and fails when the message is a raw error rather than a literal or a sanitizer; verified in both directions with a probe file. It scanned 1,277 5xx call sites clean after the change.

- `/debug/pprof` and `/metrics` disabled by default; enable via `server.enable_pprof` / `server.enable_metrics` (auth required)

## [2.4.0] - 2025-07-24

### Added

- Single-binary deployment: frontend embedded via `//go:embed`
- Docker 3-stage build (Node → Go → Alpine, ~20 MB image)
- SPA fallback middleware for client-side routing
- `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_WS_URL` for cross-domain development

### Changed

- Frontend no longer requires a separate server in production
- All API, WebSocket, and beacon endpoints served from a single port (8000)

### Fixed

- WebSocket hub race condition with `sync.Once` initialization
- Rate limiter goroutine leak with context-cancellable cleanup
- Circuit breaker goroutine leak with `Stop()` method
- SOCKS relay DB thrashing with in-memory byte accumulation
- Agent broadcast race with synchronous `broadcastAgentOnline`
- Login lockout memory leak with periodic cleanup goroutine
- Unbounded query cache replaced with bounded `TTLCache` (1000 entries, 5min TTL)

## [2.3.0] - 2025-06-15

### Added

- Frontend-backend separation with cross-domain deployment support
- CSRF protection (double-submit cookie pattern)
- SameSite=Lax cookies for session and CSRF tokens
- Configurable CORS via `allowed_origins`
- Cookie domain configuration for cross-origin sharing
- Request body limits (2MB max JSON)
- Campaign management (create, update, kill-chain tracking)
- Phishing module (templates, campaigns, credential capture)
- BloodHound integration (SharpHound collection, JSON upload/analysis)
- NTLM relay module
- Container escape module (Docker, Kubernetes detection)
- Task scheduler with cron-based scheduling
- Workflow engine with step execution
- Auto-tagging rules for agents
- Scheduled reports (daily/weekly/monthly PDF generation)
- Custom RBAC roles with per-resource permission gates
- Collaboration features (agent lock/unlock, task claiming)
- Prometheus metrics endpoint (`/metrics`)
- Log rotation with configurable retention
- Traffic profiling and monitoring
- Agent health plugins
- 40+ plugins (recon, hooks, reports, credential analysis)
- Circuit breaker for listener health monitoring
- Scripting engine (goja JavaScript runtime)
- External C2 channels (Discord, Slack)
- MITRE ATT&CK mapping (templates, timeline, phases, coverage)

### Changed

- Replaced `sync.Map` with bounded `TTLCache` for query caching
- Login lockout now uses IP-based tracking with periodic cleanup
